The **manifest-must-not-list-itself** rule is likewise untouched.
    - **New `OFFLINE.md`** at the repo root documents the whole mechanism — service worker, the two generated inventories, why paths are never hard-coded (GitHub Pages subpath), canonical-byte hashing, the working commands, and how `offline_smoke.mjs` proves offline with a negative control. Linked from `README.md`.
    - **Validation (2026-10-02):** `sync-docs.sh` re-run so `docs/pages/parent.html` mirrors the page; `check_fast.js` **7/7 green** (was 6/7 — the ZIP was the only failure); `parent_smoke.js` 7/7 with the new absence check; `hub_smoke.js` and `validate_offline.js` green inside `check_fast`; `node --check` clean. Both inventories unchanged, as expected — removing an archive cannot alter what the worker caches.
- 181. DONE — Fix `Release QA`, which failed on **every** run. Root cause found from the real log at last (task 178 needed `gh`; it is now installed and authed).
    - **`gh` is installed and authed on this host** (Alpine has no `gh` package, so `apk add gh` fails — installed the official v2.102.0 arm64 tarball to `/usr/local/bin/gh`, then `gh auth login --web`, account `radmanmilos`, scopes `gist, read:org, repo`). **The CI step logs are readable for the first time.** Two gotchas worth keeping: the device flow's waiting process is killed by a session restart and the token is then lost (no `~/.config/gh/hosts.yml` at all), so it must be started detached (`setsid`); and `gh api .../jobs/<id>/logs` refuses with "the response contains terminal escape sequences" — use `gh run view <id> --log` instead.
    - **Issue #3's pasted log was from an older run and misdescribed the failure.** The actual failure on runs #36/#38/#39/#40 is **not** a mid-session CDP socket death. It is: `Error: Chrome did not start (debug port 37177) after 4 attempts (~264s of waiting) — [attempt 1: exited=false stderr=(empty); ... ] — no assertions ran`, thrown from `headless.js:484`. The browser process was **alive on all four attempts** (`exited=false`) and its stderr was empty — it simply never opened the debug port. The "~264s" in the message is a worst-case upper bound; the step really took **27 s**.
    - **Root cause: Chrome boot crashes are ROUTINE on GitHub runners, and `Release QA` was the only runner with no retry for them.** The run #40 log proves it: **13 of the 27 matrix legs hit the identical boot crash**, each printed `----- <smoke> [boot crash, exit 1 — retrying (1/2)]`, and **all 13 passed on the retry**. Every matrix leg runs `node tools/run_all.js <smoke>`, which retries a boot crash twice. `Release QA` ran `node tools/offline_smoke.mjs` **raw** — the last asymmetry in the repo, already flagged in `AGENTS.md` — so it inherited none of that protection and turned a routine, self-healing event into a red blocking job on every single run.
    - **Two fixes, both in `tools/run_all.js`:**
      - **(a) Routing alone would NOT have worked.** The retry condition was `pass === 0 && fail === 0`, and `offline_smoke.mjs` prints **3 PASS lines** (disk + registry checks) before it ever boots a browser — so it would have been misreported as a real failure and still never retried. The boot-crash test now also honours `headless.js`'s own explicit marker: `code !== 0 && ((pass === 0 && fail === 0) || /no assertions ran/.test(out))`. The marker is emitted only by the boot-failure throw, so a genuine assertion failure cannot match it.
      - **(b) The runner could not even be pointed at the file.** `run_all.js` mapped positional names to `<name>.js` and then filtered on `_smoke.js`, so `offline_smoke.mjs` was rejected outright. It now accepts an explicit `.mjs` filename (`/_smoke\.m?js$/`). **The battery is deliberately unchanged at 27 tools** — `--list`/`--list --json` are untouched, so CI's generated matrix does not gain a ~1-minute gate leg.
      - **`ci.yml`** now runs `node tools/run_all.js offline_smoke.mjs --concurrency 1` in the `Offline E2E` step. `validate_workflow.js` 11/11 after the edit.
    - **⚠ A SECOND, WORSE BUG found while validating: `offline_smoke.mjs` had NO exit code at all.** It fell off the end of its IIFE with status 0, so every `FAIL` it printed was cosmetic — the blocking release gate could only ever go red by *crashing*, never by *failing an assertion*. Observed directly: on a run where 3 checks failed it printed them and still exited 0. (It now exits 1 via `getFails()`.) This is the same family as every "a check that cannot fail" in this repo's history, and it is the more dangerous half: on any host where `racing3d` cannot boot, the offline gate reported failures and reported success. **Consequence to watch on the next CI run:** this gate had never once completed on the runner, so it has never actually been proven green — a real assertion failure may now surface for the first time. That is the correct outcome, not a regression.
    - **Reproduced the CI failure locally, then proved the fix.** A `CHROME_PATH` shim that `exec sleep 600` is exactly the observed shape (alive, no port, empty stderr): before the fix `run_all.js` exited 1 with `3 pass / 0 fail` and **no retry**; after it, the same shim yields `[boot crash, exit 1 — retrying (1/2)]`, `(2/2)`, then a final verdict. Negative-tested by reverting the `no assertions ran` clause: the retry disappears and it fails immediately, so the guard discriminates.
    - **Not a regression: 3 local `racing3d`/WebGL failures.** `P4 racing3d loads and boots offline [timed out waiting for racing3d boot]`, the acceptance line, and a runtime-error check (10 captured `THREE.WebGLRenderer: A WebGL context could not be created`). Verified **identical at clean HEAD `c407fcd`** by stashing all work, so it is the documented no-WebGL limitation of this host — not a regression. Do not "fix" it by stubbing WebGL; the runner has software WebGL and `racing3d_smoke` is 33/33 there.
    - **Validation (2026-10-02):** `check_fast.js` 7/7 green; `validate_workflow.js` 11/11; `run_all.js --list` still 27 tools; `offline_smoke.mjs` now exits 1 on the known local WebGL failure; boot-crash retry negative-tested.
    - **✅ VERIFIED ON RUN #41 — CI IS GREEN, ISSUE #3 CLOSED.** `Release QA` passed for the first time in the repo's history: **29 jobs succeeded, 1 skipped (`Extended`, manual/weekly by design), 0 failed.** The proof that the fix did the work rather than the flakiness simply passing: **run #41 hit a boot crash again**, the retry fired, and the gate then completed — `[boot crash, exit 1 — retrying (1/2)]` → `[exit 0, 103 pass / 0 fail, 67.4s]`, `PASS acceptance: every offline game loads, plays and returns (16 games) [all green]`, accessibility `critical=0, serious=0, moderate=0, minor=0`. Note `racing3d` **did** pass offline on the runner (103 checks), confirming the 3 local WebGL failures are host-only. **Issue #3 closed** with the root cause and the "do not apply the suggested fix" rationale recorded in the closing comment.
- 180. DONE — Fix the `games_map_negtest` false claim that redlined Release QA on runs #34 and #37. (Completed 2026-10-02, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - **Found while running the guards for task 179, and reproducible at clean HEAD `359085b`** (verified by stashing all work): `node tools/guards/games_map_negtest.js` exits 1 with `unreachable: audio_buses_smoke`. That step is `Release QA`'s "Game-file smoke mapping guard (negative test)", which is the failing step on CI runs **#34 and #37** — so this is a live, recurring Release QA failure, and it was masking the Offline E2E failure on those two runs.
    - **The fault was in the CHECK, not in the coverage.** `audio_buses_smoke.js` covers `game/shared/audio-buses.js` (R15/task 175, loaded by 13 pages), and `mapFileToSmokes()` routes **every** `game/shared/*` edit to `BROAD()` — the whole battery. So its coverage is real and complete. But `games_map_negtest.js` asserts "every per-game smoke is reachable via pages or game files", and that smoke is reachable from **neither**: it is not a registry page's `smoke` and no file in `game/games/` resolves to it. The guard was making a claim its own implementation could never satisfy. **Adding `audio-buses` to `run_all.js`'s tables would have been the wrong fix** — it would have invented a mapping that does not exist to satisfy a check that was simply wrong.
    - **Fixed in the guard:** `audio_buses_smoke` joins `NON_GAME_SMOKES` with the reason recorded, and **two new checks keep the exclusion honest** — the `BROAD()` fallback must still exist (it is what justifies excluding any `game/shared/*` smoke), and every name in `NON_GAME_SMOKES` must be a real file. Without those, the list becomes a place to hide a genuinely unreachable smoke and the guard would pass with coverage lost.
    - **Validation (2026-10-02):** guard exits 0, "the derived game-file mapping is complete and every check can fail"; **negative-tested** by removing `audio_buses_smoke` from `NON_GAME_SMOKES` (i.e. the exact HEAD state) — it fails again with exit 1, so the guard genuinely discriminates. `route_contract_negtest` 6/6 and `registry_guards_negtest` 12/12 also green. `check_fast.js` 7/7.
- 178. DONE — Release QA's diagnostic could name the wrong Chrome (the one piece of evidence run #39 had). Why: the CDP failure message is the only record of *why* the browser went away, and it was reading a loop-wide variable that any earlier boot attempt's dying child can also write. Shipped in `c407fcd`.
    - **Started from issue #3, not from a guess.** `radmanmilos/Games-for-kids#3` ("failing test in CI") is the only open issue. Verified against the API rather than taken on faith: run **#39** on `359085b` = **30 jobs, 27 succeeded, 1 skipped (`Extended`, correctly manual/weekly), 1 failure (`Release QA`)**. The **entire 27-leg smoke matrix is green**; this is the only red job, and it has failed on every execution (runs #31–#34, #37–#39).
    - **The issue's proposed fix is wrong and must not be applied.** It recommends "Option 1: Add Connection Retry Logic (Recommended)" and offers "fix or remove this test". Both regress: the CDP handshake is *already* inside the `BOOT_ATTEMPTS` retry loop (task 177), so another retry layer cannot help a socket that dies **mid-session**, which is the observed shape; and `offline_smoke.mjs` is the only thing proving offline playability — it is the gate that found 3 real product bugs in R6. Removing it deletes coverage. Its root-cause claim ("Chrome under heavy load / unstable debugging port") is a guess contradicted by nothing in evidence.
    - **What the log actually says, and why it proves nothing.** The crash is `ws.onclose` → `fail()` with `code=1006 wasClean=false preceded-by=(nothing logged) browser=Chrome exited code=0 signal=null`. That `browser=Chrome exited` clause is task 177d's own diagnostic, and it is the **only** evidence of why the browser vanished — so it has to be trustworthy. It is not: `describe()` read the boot loop's shared `chromeExitEvent`, which every attempt writes (including a failed attempt's child still winding down) and which is **never cleared** when a later attempt succeeds. The line could describe a corpse from attempt 1 while the live browser was perfectly healthy. Frames confirm it is current code (`headless.js:238` = `new Error(why)`, `:303` = the `fail()` call in `ws.onclose`).
    - **RESOLVED in `headless.js`:** the exit record is now a per-iteration `let exitEvent` inside the boot loop, and `describe()` reads that; `chromeExitEvent` is still assigned (`chromeExitEvent = exitEvent`) so the boot-failure report is untouched. The "still running" branch now names the live pid (`Chrome still running (pid N)`), which the shared-variable version could not do.
    - **The guard, and a vacuous check caught before it shipped (`tools/guards/chrome_diagnostic_negtest.js`, 10 checks).** A shim `CHROME_PATH` exits 7 on the first boot attempt and `exec`s the real Chromium afterwards, so a stale record genuinely exists; the live browser is then killed under an in-flight `Runtime.evaluate` (an idle socket death is silently swallowed, so the failure must be reproduced with work in flight — which is also what Release QA actually was).
    - **⚠ The first version of that guard passed against the pre-fix code.** Its assertion was behavioural, and which of `describe()`'s two truthful branches appears depends on whether the CDP socket close or the child `exit` event lands first — a per-run race. It was rewritten so the **regression assertion is deterministic source shape** (5 checks: `describe()` is inside the boot loop's per-iteration binding and cannot reach the loop-wide variable), with the behavioural half demoted to supporting evidence that must hold either way. Negative-tested by reverting `describe()`: **`9 passed, 1 failed`, exit 1**. Restoring the fix returns 10/10. This is the task-177 lesson again in a new shape: *the harness telling you why it broke is itself a check, and it needed its own check.*
    - **⚠ Two further self-inflicted test bugs found while building it, both of which would have produced a green guard:** attaching `.catch()` to the in-flight `evalp` made the rejection **handled**, so the absorber never printed and the assertion could never fire (the absorber only speaks for *unhandled* rejections); and the poll/report regex was loose enough to match the separate `[headless] Chrome exited while a CDP session was open:` line, i.e. the test was reading a different message than it thought.
    - **Validation (2026-10-02):** `guards/chrome_diagnostic_negtest.js` 10/10; the sibling `guards/cdp_fail_negtest.js` 7/7 ("0 failure(s)") and now emits `browser=Chrome still running (pid 24132)`; `run_all.js sw_update_smoke.js` 9/9; `run_all.js parent_smoke.js` 7/7; `check_fast.js` = **6 of 7 stages green**, the single failure being the pre-existing untracked `docs/game-offline.zip` described in the handover (unrelated, present before this task). `node --check` clean on both edited files. **That red stage was resolved later the same day by task 179, which removed the ZIP option entirely; `check_fast.js` is 7/7.**
    - **⚠ SUPERSEDED BY TASK 181 — this task did not fix run #39, and its own evidence turned out to describe a different failure.** Task 178 made the diagnostic trustworthy but established no root cause, as recorded below. Once `gh` was authed, the real run #39/#40 log showed the error was a **boot** crash (`Chrome did not start ... no assertions ran`, browser alive, port silent), **not** the mid-session CDP socket death this task was chasing. The fix here still stands on its own (a diagnostic that can name the wrong process is worth fixing), but the CI failure was task 181.
- **Task 177d — `offline_smoke` still dies opaquely on the runner: the CDP `fail()` path itself kills the process** (Ponytail Lazy Dev, DONE 2026-10-02 — diagnosed and fixed the CDP disconnect rejection storm; why: preserve a useful failure report and nonzero status instead of an opaque Node crash or false green).
    - **Run #34** (`5414e0d`, carrying the task-177c fix): the smoke matrix went green **in full** for the first time, including `driving_smoke.js` and `kitty_smoke.js`, which confirms both 177c fixes hold on the Linux runner and the explorer P4 failure is gone. `Release QA` then failed: `Error: CDP websocket closed unexpectedly (ws://127.0.0.1:44583/...)` from `headless.js:178`, ~37 s in, immediately after the 3 pre-browser checks, with **no browser check ever running**. This is the run-#31 "Release QA is the only place Chrome dies" mode returning unchanged — a **harness** defect, not a product one (the tool is exit 0 locally, all 16 games played).
    - **Key evidence: Chrome did NOT die.** The task-177 diagnostic never fired — there is **no `[headless] Chrome exited while a CDP session was open` line** — so `child.on('exit')` never ran, the browser process stayed alive, and only the **websocket** closed. The stderr/exit evidence added in `aac8681` is therefore still not reaching the log, i.e. the mid-session path keeps losing the very evidence it was added to collect.
    - **Root cause — `fail()` is itself an unhandled-rejection factory (headless.js:177-182).** `ws.onclose` → `fail()` rejects **every** in-flight command in `pending`, then clears the map. Each promise it rejects that no caller is currently awaiting becomes an **unhandled rejection**, and Node kills the process with a non-zero exit printing only `for (const [, p] of pending) p.rej(new Error(why))` plus the socket URL — precisely the observed output. It also explains why no existing guard helps: the process is killed by *Node*, not exited by the tool, so `run_all.js`/`check_fast.js`'s `pass=0 fail=0` boot-crash retry can never see it, and the crash message is a symptom of the rejection storm rather than evidence about why the socket closed.
    - **RESOLVED:** CDP socket failure now uses one tagged error for all pending calls, clears their timers, and reports a single browser/socket diagnostic while setting `process.exitCode = 1`; ordinary unhandled rejections still crash normally. `ws.onerror` records the cause and gives `ws.onclose` time to supply the close code. `tools/guards/cdp_fail_negtest.js` exercises a real disconnect, checks that cleanup completes with one diagnostic and a failed exit, verifies ordinary unhandled errors remain fatal, and removes the guard to prove the old crash returns. It restores `tools/headless.js` after mutation.
    - **Validation (2026-10-02):** `node tools/guards/cdp_fail_negtest.js` passed 7/7 checks; `node tools/offline_smoke.mjs` passed the full offline flow for all 16 games, including runtime-error checks; `node tools/check_fast.js` passed all 7 stages; `node --check` passed both edited JS files. The pre-browser disconnect now reports its close code, preceding websocket error, Chrome exit state, and stderr tail instead of killing Node at `fail()`.
    - `Release QA` runs `node tools/offline_smoke.mjs` **raw** (ci.yml:130), the one runner still bypassing `run_all.js`; this harness fix makes the CDP fatal failure visible as a normal nonzero tool exit for that path.
    - **Mechanism proven locally (throwaway probe, reverted):** replaying headless.js:177-182 with one awaited and one un-awaited command in `pending` printed `awaited command rejected cleanly: CDP websocket closed unexpectedly` and then died with `Error: CDP websocket closed unexpectedly  at fail (…:7:60)` — **the same message and the same `at fail` frame as the CI log** — and never reached the next line. So the crash output is a symptom of the rejection storm, not an explanation of the socket close.
- **Task 177c — Release QA: a real product bug (explorer controls off-screen) found and fixed** (Ponytail Lazy Dev, 2026-10-02, DONE).
    - **RESOLVED.** The P4 explorer geometry failure was a **genuine product bug**, not a runner artifact: `explorer.html` was migrated from `resources/papper_kitty.html` (canvas 70% + green `#controls` bar 30% + an absolutely-positioned `#score-board`) to a full-bleed canvas + overlay HUD, but only `.hud-btn`/`.back-btn` got `position:absolute`. `.btn` (`#btn-left/right/jump`) has no placement, so it stayed in normal flow **after the 100%-height canvas inside `#app{position:fixed;inset:0;overflow:hidden}`** and rendered below the fold on **every** viewport — a child could not move or jump. The `.hud` labels had the same fault via **stale ID selectors** (`#hud-world/#hud-level/#hud-coins`, but the markup uses `#world-name/#level-num/#coin-count`), so the world/level/coin labels were off-screen too. Offline smoke passed locally only because `boxOf`'s `scrollIntoView({block:'center'})` scrolls the clipped `#app` and clamped the button exactly at the fold (`bottom` = innerHeight); on the runner the `.btn-jump` 6% `pulse` scale tipped it 1 px over (127 px = 120 px × 1.06), i.e. the same task-120 anti-pattern — a test rescuing geometry no child could reach. `kitty_smoke.js` asserted `.char-btn` geometry but never `.btn`/`.hud`.
    - **Fix (`game/pages/explorer.html`):** overlay the three controls at the bottom like the sibling `.adv-btn` games — `.btn{position:absolute;bottom:calc(2vmin + env(safe-area-inset-bottom));z-index:10}`, `◀`/`▶` at `left:2vmin`/`calc(4vmin + 100px)`, `🐾` at `right:2vmin`; raised `🌍`/`🔊` above the 120 px jump (`calc(2vmin + 120px + 3vmin)` / `+ 15vmin`) so they no longer collide with it; and repointed the HUD selectors at the real IDs (`#world-name` top-centre, `#level-num` `left:14vmin` clear of the back button, `#coin-count` top-right).
    - **Coverage added (`tools/kitty_smoke.js`):** after the character is chosen (readiness wait for the picker to close), assert `#btn-left/right/jump` are fully in-viewport **and** the topmost element at their centre (`elementFromPoint` — a real tap, not `.click()`), and that the `pointer-events:none` HUD labels are fully on-screen. This closes the exact gap that let the bug ship.
    - **Run #33 (`1a88145`) turned out to be a different blocker:** `Release QA` was `skipped` because `driving_smoke.js` failed first on `steering: up decreases y, right increases offsetX [{"y":461,"ox":176} -> {"y":461,"ox":176}]` (no movement). Reproduced locally as a **pass** (`461,176 → 419,218`), so it is a **timing race**: the test held the keys and slept a fixed 150 ms hoping the rAF loop had ticked, but `updateDrive` moves a fixed step *per `update()` call* and a headless browser under CI load can deliver zero frames in 150 ms. Fixed the honest way (never a longer sleep): `a.setPaused(true)` + 5 explicit `a.update()` ticks + `setPaused(false)`, the same deterministic stepping `racing3d_smoke` uses.
    - **Validation:** `kitty_smoke.js` passed including the two new geometry checks; `driving_smoke.js` passed deterministic steering ticks; `check_fast.js` passed 7/7; `offline_smoke.mjs` played all 16 games with no runtime errors. Run #34's complete smoke matrix confirmed these fixes on CI. Committed and pushed in `5414e0d`; Release QA's remaining disconnect failure was isolated and fixed in task 177d.
    - **Run #32** (`efb98f1`, 36989560943): Chrome booted fine and stayed up — the run-#31 crash modes (`CDP websocket closed`, `never opened debug port`) did **not** reproduce; the `aac8681` diagnostics never fired. The offline E2E got much further and hit a **product-side geometry failure instead**: `FAIL P4 explorer primary interaction offline  [outside the viewport (-3,674 127x127)]`. Local `offline_smoke.mjs` still plays explorer 16/16 with the button stably centred at `(508,377)` 120x123 across frames. So this is a **runner-layout divergence, not a crash**.
    - **Read of the numbers:** `127px = 12vmin` ⇒ `vmin ≈ 10.58` ⇒ min(vw,vh) ≈ **1058**, not 800 — the runner's effective viewport/layout at tap time was **not** the harness-set 1280x800. x=-3 (3px left of the viewport edge) is a failed `left < 0` check, not a marginal rounding.
    - **Diagnosis committed (headless `boxOf` enriched):** the harness re-checks the same button's geometry (window, dpr, scroll, display/position of element AND parent, vmin) and folds it into the `outside the viewport` failure message, so **run #33 reports the runner's real layout instead of us guessing**. `node --check` clean; `offline_smoke.mjs` still 16/16 locally.
    - **Hypotheses to resolve with that evidence:** (a) the P4 transition flips layout/viewport ordering (parent→game `location.href` navigation inside the single P2-offline session with `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` still active); (b) a late-arriving stylesheet/image (offline cache priming vs SW serve ordering) relayouts the modal after the tap target is resolved; (c) `#app{overflow:hidden}` + `boxOf`'s `scrollIntoView({block:'center'})` scrolled the containing block. The enriched message discriminates all three.
    - **Diagnosis, not guesswork.** GitHub's job-log endpoint returns `403` unauthenticated even for a public repo and the log HTML says "Sign in to view logs", so the first 25 runs were undiagnosable. With `gh` authenticated (`gh auth login --web` device flow) the log bodies settled it in one read. Every run #1–#25 has concluded `failure` — CI was never green, so there was no good commit to bisect against and the cause was structural.
    - **Shape 1 (fast, ~26 s):** the log ends `counting_smoke crashed: Error: Chrome did not start (debug port 34903) after 4 attempts (~24s of waiting) — no assertions ran` at `headless.js:246`. That is the boot budget (`BOOT_ATTEMPTS 4 × BOOT_POLL_TRIES 30 × BOOT_POLL_MS 200` = 24 s + 3 pauses) matched almost exactly, and 19/26 legs failed this way with Chrome printing nothing at all.
    - **Shape 2 (silent, 612 s):** `ocean_smoke.js` printed **zero output** for the whole 10-minute step timeout and was then reported as "Terminate orphan process: node" — no assertion, no diagnostic. Root cause: `cdp()` resolved only on `ws.onopen`, with no `onerror`, no `onclose` and no timeout, and `send()` returned a promise that only settled from a message id that might never arrive, so a refused or lost DevTools connection hung the process forever. **An unbounded await is a check that cannot fail.**
    - **Fixes in `tools/headless.js`:** `CDP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` 15 s, `CDP_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` 30 s, `BOOT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` 2 s (so one `/json/version` request cannot outlive the poll budget its own error message counts against), the handshake moved **inside** the existing `BOOT_ATTEMPTS` retry loop so a refused websocket is retried like a slow port, the teardown poll bounded too, and two standard CI-Chrome flags: `--disable-dev-shm-usage` (a runner's 64 MB `/dev/shm` is not enough; the symptom is a browser that never appears) and `--remote-allow-origins=*` (Chrome ≥ 111 rejects an `Origin`-bearing DevTools websocket otherwise).
    - **Fix in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`:** the matrix ran `node tools/<smoke>` **raw**, bypassing the 0-check boot-crash retry that both `run_all.js` and `check_fast.js` already apply — the only such asymmetry in the repo, and why `hub_smoke` in the un-contended `fast` job never once failed while 19/26 parallel legs did. Each leg now runs `node tools/run_all.js <smoke>`.
    - **Validation:** `cdp()` proved bounded with a throwaway probe against a refused port (rejects in 28 ms) and a black-hole port that accepts then says nothing (**15015 ms** = exactly `CDP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS`); probe reverted. The `run_all.js` routing was **negative-tested** — a deliberately broken assertion gives `pass=15 fail=1`, is *not* retried and exits 1, so the change cannot hide a regression. `validate_workflow.js` 11/11; `check_fast.js` 7/7; full battery **27/27 tools, 570 checks, 0 failures**. `game/` unchanged, so `docs/` and the offline inventories were already current.
    - **VERIFIED ON RUN #27** (`a2bc0a3`): **27 jobs succeeded, 1 failed, 1 skipped**, against 19 failed legs + a 313 s `fast` job before. The `fast` job went **313 s → 41 s** — the unbounded await is gone for good, since the 300 s per-stage `spawnSync` timeout no longer fires. Legs finish in 34–66 s instead of failing at 26 s or hanging 612 s, and the boot-crash retry **demonstrably fired and recovered** (`racing3d_smoke.js [boot crash, exit 1 — retrying (1/2)]`, then a real run). `Release QA` is `skipped`, not failed — correctly gated behind `needs: [fast, smoke]`.
    - **The one red leg, `racing3d_smoke.js`, was only fixable with evidence from the runner** — local was green, so a local-only red meant nothing. The user chose diagnostics-first. Three CI-only defects surfaced and were fixed in order, each confirmed by the next run: **(a)** software-WebGL frames cost 113–183 ms on the runner, so `Math.min(delta, 0.05)` bound the rAF delta on *every* frame and the game ran at ~41% of wall-clock speed (`frames:15, simMs:700, worstMs:183` vs local `frames:100, simMs:1767, worstMs:33`) — steering/bank checks now use `haltLoop(true)` + fixed `step(1/60)` budgets (`d9bf602`); **(b)** a flat 30 s `Runtime.evaluate` timeout misclassified a *slow* page as a *wedged* harness (`pass=23 fail=0` at 30 s) — split into `CDP_EVALUATE_TIMEOUT_MS=90000` (`220dae2`); **(c)** the particle check read `total` at a fixed step 120, but the lap line is crossed at step 36 on the runner (53 locally) and the petals die ~70–85 steps later — `{crossStep:36, atCross:24, celeAtCross:24, cele:0, total:0}` showed the burst fired but expired before the sample. Assertions moved to the burst itself: `celeAtCross>0`, `spinAtCross>0`, plus a running `maxTotal` so the cap is still enforced across the window.
    - **VERIFIED ON RUN #31** (`f65deee`): the entire 26-leg smoke matrix is green on the runner — `racing3d_smoke.js` 33/33 with the same checks that were failing, and **28 jobs succeeded / 1 failed / 1 skipped**. The remaining failure is the first *ever* execution of `Release QA` (it was always skipped while the matrix was red): its `Offline E2E` step crashed with `CDP websocket closed unexpectedly (ws://127.0.0.1:...)` in `offline_smoke.mjs` after 3 checks. Locally the same tool plays all 16 games (exit 0), so this is a **runner-environment crash, not an offline regression** — to be characterized by a rerun before anything is "fixed".
    - **WHAT THE RERUN SHOWED (same run #31, job 2):** `node tools/offline_smoke.mjs` again failed after the 3 pre-browser checks, this time `Chrome did not start (debug port 41383) after 4 attempts`. Critical read: the step only took **~25 s wall-clock** even though the error message claims "~264s of waiting" — that message is a *worst-case upper bound* (4 attempts × 30 polls × 2200 ms), but a refused debug port makes each `fetch` fail instantly, so 4 × 30 × (200 + tiny) ≈ 24 s. The two failures are **two different crash modes in the same job class**: run #31 initial = Chrome booted then **died mid-session** (~44 s in, webSocket closed); rerun = Chrome **never opened the debug port at all** on any of 4 attempts. All 26 smoke legs booted Chrome fine on their own runners the same run — the Release QA step is the only place Chrome dies. **Diagnosis needs runner-side evidence**: the boot loop in `tools/headless.js` spawned the child with `execFile(chromeBin, CHROME_FLAGS)` and **no stdio and no exit handler**, so Chrome's own stderr (why it crashed/killed) is invisible; the mid-session websocket death prints nothing about the process. Both diagnostic blind spots are why the two modes "hid". AGENTS.md already carried the stale bullet claiming `offline_smoke.mjs` "does not currently test offline" — the tool was rewritten for R6 (single session, CDP `Network.emulateNetworkConditions`, `?nocache=` negative control, R7 registry-driven interaction specs) and `ci.yml` blocked release on it; the AGENTS bullet was corrected to match.
    - **Diagnostics committed (`aac8681`):** the boot loop now captures Chrome's stderr (capped 2 KB/attempt, tail in the error) and per-attempt exit-event state into the throw message itself, and logs `[headless] Chrome exited while a CDP session was open: code=… signal=… (tag …)` + stderr tail to the tool's stderr if the process dies mid-session. `close()` sets `closingDown` first so the deliberate teardown kill is never misreported as a crash (validated: zero `[headless]` lines on the happy path). Local: `offline_smoke.mjs` still 16/16, `parent_smoke` green, `node --check` clean. **CI run #32 will collect the stderr/exit evidence and point at the actual cause.**
    - **Remaining, separate problem:** `racing3d_smoke.js` was the one red leg — 28 pass / 5 fail. It runs at all, so the runner *does* have software WebGL and the documented no-WebGL `SKIP` path never triggers.
- **Task 177b — racing3d's 5 CI-only failures, diagnosed with evidence then fixed** (Ponytail Lazy Dev, 2026-10-02).
    - **Instrumented first (commit `f836244`), as agreed.** Added a page-side `__probe` (real frame count + the sim time those frames actually delivered, using the game's own 50 ms clamp) plus per-check payloads, and made **no** assertion change. The user chose evidence over a blind fix precisely because the failure does not reproduce on the Windows host.
    - **Run #28 evidence — one root cause, one latent bug.** `probe` on the runner: `{frames: 15, simMs: 700, worstMs: 183}`; locally `{frames: 100, simMs: 1767, worstMs: 33}`. The runner delivers 15 frames of ~113 ms, so the dt clamp binds every frame and the game runs at **~41% of wall-clock speed** — 700 ms of sim for 1700 ms of wall clock. Every `await sleep(N)` in the smoke therefore under-bought on CI while passing locally **with identical game code**. The bank check is the clean arithmetic proof: `steerYaw` returns as `exp(-2.8·t)`, so 0.417 → below 0.05 needs ~757 ms of *sim*, and CI's `sleep(1300)` bought only ~540 ms, leaving 0.121.
    - **Fix 1 — the two wall-clock physics checks now step a fixed dt** (`haltLoop(true)` + `step(1/60)`), using the exact sim budgets the old millisecond sleeps bought (400/150/250/900/300/400 → 24/9/15/54/18/24 steps; 400/1300 → 24/78). Same thresholds, deterministic on any host, and the deterministic pattern batches 4–9 already used.
    - **Fix 2 — the decor check had two host-dependencies.** It chose its billboard with `decorNear(progress() + 0.05)`, i.e. wherever the kart happened to be, so the hosts picked **different items** (`idx 6, t 0.411` locally vs `idx 4, t 0.279` on the runner) whose pulses peak a different number of steps after the seek; and it asserted `ampInit === 0`, which asserts the **host's** `prefers-reduced-motion`, not a product behaviour (`REDUCED_MOTION` is read once at module load, so no smoke can set it — the runner reported `rm: false`). Now the item is chosen by index and the gate is asserted as `decorAmp === (rm ? 0 : 1)`, which still fails if the gate logic breaks and holds on any host.
    - **Fix 3 — the oscillation, which invalidated the original assertion.** `racing3d.mjs:2295` re-triggers `pulseAge = 0` on *every* frame the kart is within 10 u, so the scale swings 1 ↔ 1.15 continuously while nearby and only decays after the kart leaves. `1.14095` is literally `1 + 0.15·sin(phase)`, which is why two different hosts sampled the *identical* value — that coincidence is what disproved "the decay is slow". Baseline is now asserted only after an explicit clearance condition, using the game's own `Math.min` two-sided distance (a forward-only test passes vacuously, since seeking *behind* an item still counts as near), plus 24 steps (0.4 s) for the final 0.3 s pulse to finish. `clear === true` is itself asserted, so a kart that never leaves the radius reports instead of silently passing.
    - **Locally: 33/33 pass and `check_fast.js` is 7/7 green.**
    - **Run #29 verified the racing3d fix on the Linux runner**: `reactive decor` now **passes there** — `{"idx":3,"peak":1.15,"atClear":1,"endOn":1,"ampInit":1,"rm":false,"steps":256,"clear":true}` — so the deterministic stepping and `decorAmp === (rm ? 0 : 1)` both hold with reduced motion **off** (`rm: false`, `ampInit: 1`), which is the half that could never be verified on the Windows host. The other 22 checks up to that point also passed, including `steering` and `kart banks`. `steps: 256` on the runner vs 244 locally shows the clearance wait is only mildly entry-speed sensitive against a 900-step cap.
    - **Run #29 then exposed a different defect, in the harness: `pass=23 fail=0`, exit 1, with `CDP Runtime.evaluate (id 61) got no response within 30000ms`.** The block that hung is trivial (12 steps of the contact-shadow batch), so this was not a bad assertion — it is the new 30 s command bound misfiring. **`Runtime.evaluate` is the only CDP method whose cost scales with how fast the *page* draws** (it must be scheduled on a main thread the render loop holds for a whole frame), and on this software-WebGL runner frames cost 113–183 ms. A flat bound turns "slow page" into "broken harness" and kills the whole smoke. **Fixed by splitting the budget:** `CDP_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` stays 30 s for the control plane (navigation/attach, where a real wedge lives and where the connect fix applies) and `CDP_EVALUATE_TIMEOUT_MS = 90000` covers page evaluation. This does **not** reopen the run-#25 hang, which was an unbounded *connect*.
    - **Run #30 verified the timeout split and narrowed the leg to 2 checks: 31 pass / 2 fail.** `steering`, `kart banks` and `reactive decor` are now **green on the runner**, and the 90 s evaluate budget stopped the misfire (the leg simply ran to completion, 180 s). The boot-crash retry fired again and recovered (`[boot crash, exit 1 — retrying (1/2)]`).
    - **The last two failures were answered by the `f836244` diagnostics, which is exactly what they were added for.** Run #30 payload: `{"lap0":1,"lap1":2,"cele":0,"total":0,"spinning":0,"crossStep":36,"atCross":24,"celeAtCross":24,"pre":6}`. So the burst **did** fire — 24 `celebrate` petals spawned at the lap cross — and every one had expired by the time the check sampled. The lap line is crossed at whatever step the entry speed reaches it (**36** on the runner vs **53** locally), and the petals all die about 70–85 steps after spawning, so reading the count at a fixed step 120 meant the runner (which crosses earlier, therefore samples latest) saw `total: 0` while the burst was demonstrably real. **Fixed by asserting the same behaviour where it happens**: `celeAtCross > 0` and `spinAtCross > 0` at the burst, with a running `maxTotal` so the `MAX_PARTICLES` cap is still checked across the whole window instead of at one instant. Run #30 had already recorded `celeAtCross: 24` on the runner, so this is verified against observed CI data rather than assumed.
- 176. DONE — Regression guards for the two GitHub code-scanning alerts. (Completed 2026-10-02, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - Advanced Security opened two alerts against this repo and Copilot Autofix fixed them on 2026-10-01 (`32c7e48`, `fc4c1ce`): **alert 7 "Incomplete URL scheme check"** — the reference filter in `validate_offline.js` excluded `data:`, `mailto:` and `javascript:` but not `vbscript:`, i.e. a partial denylist; **alert 8 "Bad HTML filtering regexp"** — the `<script>`/`<style>` stripper in `audit_serbian_strings.js` ended its closing tag at `<\/script\s*>`, which `</script foo="bar">` walks straight through, so the "visible text" it computed could still contain script bodies. A fix with no guard comes back the next time somebody writes the same shape.
    - Added `tools/check_scan_alerts.js`, 5 read-only checks over every `.js`/`.mjs` in `game/` + `tools/`: (1) every dangerous-scheme filter must test `javascript:` **and** `vbscript:`; (2) every `.replace()` that strips a `<script>`/`<style>` element must consume the whole closing tag across six spellings (`</tag>`, `</tag >`, `</tag\t>`, `</tag\n>`, `</tag foo="bar">`, `</tag data-x=1 >`). Rule 2 is proved **behaviourally** — replace the element, mask it, require the body to be gone — so a different-but-correct rewrite also passes instead of only the autofix's exact text.
    - Both checkers are self-tested against the pre-autofix shapes held in memory and must **reject** them, and the two repo-wide scans must each find at least one site, so the guard cannot report green by matching nothing. Wired into `check_fast.js` as a 7th stage, so CI's `fast` job now runs it.
    - Known residual, recorded in the tool header rather than silently widened: `</script/>` needs whitespace after the tag name and is not covered by the tolerant `(?:\s+[^>]*)?` group, so that pattern is left exactly as the autofix wrote it.
    - **Validation:** `check_scan_alerts.js` 5/5 across 94 files; **negative-tested against the real files** — reverting either autofix makes it fail, naming `tools/validate_offline.js:139 (missing vbscript:)` and `tools/audit_serbian_strings.js:96`, exit 1; `check_fast.js` **7/7 green in 9.6 s**. `game/` is unchanged, so the `docs/` mirror and the offline inventories were already current (`validate_generated` green) and `sync-docs.sh` was a no-op.
    - **Follow-up (2026-10-06, CodeQL `js/incomplete-multi-character-sanitization` on `tools/validate_pages.js:61`):** Rule 2 had a blind spot — it only scanned strippers whose regex *mentions a `</script>`/`</style>` closing tag*, so the **generic** `m[2].replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '')` in `validate_pages.js` evaded it and CodeQL flagged it as incomplete sanitization: `[^>]*` can match across `<<scr<script>ipt>>`, and one global pass removes the inner `<script>` leaving a fresh `<script>` behind. Fixed two ways: (a) `validate_pages.js` replaced the regex with a **char scan** (toggle `inTag` on `<`/`>`), which needs no regex and cannot be fooled; (b) Rule 2 widened to any `.replace(/<…` literal (`site.body.startsWith('<')`), so a generic `<[^>]*>`-style stripper is now rejected too (it consumes only up to the first `>`, not whole elements) — plus a new Rule-3 negative test feeding `BAD_GENERIC_FILTER_SAMPLE` through the checker and requiring rejection. Guard now 6/6 across 110 files; repo-wide scan confirms no `<[^>]*>`-style stripper remains. Any future "strip the tags" code must use the char scan or a whole-element pattern.

Restored the file from `4df14bc^`, dropping only the 2D-racer-only `RACING_CHARACTERS` block (its consumer `racing.js` was deleted in the same task); `racing3d.mjs` only reads `worlds`/`music`. `generate_sw_list.js` walks `game/`, so the service-worker cache list, the 207-entry offline manifest and the `docs/` mirror all picked it up automatically (the stale `games/racing.js` entry is now gone).

The harness runs `--disable-gpu`; Alpine's Chromium 136 ships no SwiftShader, and ANGLE's Vulkan backend aborts with `SwANGLE failed with error EGL_NOT_INITIALIZED` / `VK_KHR_surface` not supported. `--use-angle=swiftshader`, `--use-angle=swrast`, `--use-gl=egl` and installing `mesa-egl` were all tried and none produce a context. The game correctly takes its own "3D није доступан овде" fallback.
        - **How it is handled now (the important part):** `racing3d_smoke.js` probes for a WebGL context first. With one, all 32 original checks run unchanged. Without one it runs 3 real checks — the fallback message, page chrome, and **the shared `RACING_CONFIG` load (8 worlds / 8 music / 3 obstacle types)** — and reports the whole race battery as a counted `SKIP` via the new `skip()`/`getSkips()` in `tools/headless.js`, exiting non-zero only if a real check fails. A skip prints `SKIP` and is counted; it is never a pass.
        - **The masking fix that was reverted:** an earlier attempt added a fake `window.__r3d` stub in racing3d's WebGL-catch path and made the smoke print `ALL CHECKS PASSED` and exit 0 when WebGL was missing. That is the task-120 anti-pattern (stubbing the code under test) and would have hidden this regression permanently. Both were reverted. Because the config check runs in *both* modes, the regression that was actually found is now guarded even where the race cannot run.
        - **Accepted consequence:** the 3D race has **no automated coverage on a WebGL-less host**. The full battery only runs on a GPU/SwiftShader host (CI runners). The user must play-test racing3d on a real device; that is already an open item in `HANDOVER_PROMPT.md`.

Only `--concurrency=6` worked. Fixed by walking the args with an index and consuming the value (`i++`). This is the same class of bug as task 148's `screenshot.js` and task 150's `axe_check.js` `window` mock: **a documented entry point that silently doesn't work.** Worth re-auditing the other documented commands.

That keeps the "exactly one coin" guarantee intact — it is now deterministic instead of accidentally loose.

Fix: poll until the hub screen is actually present (up to 6s) and report the landed URL.
        - `hub_smoke` "games tile opens games sub-hub" flaked the same way in the first parallel attempt but has been green in every run since; left as-is rather than changing a passing assertion.
