- 153. DONE — R2: Offline Inventory Truth and Cache Generation. (2026-09-30, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - **Roadmap's "current issue" is already stale — verified, not assumed.** It claims the cache list is missing `data/app-registry.js`, `pages/explorer.html`, `pages/parent.html` and `docs/OFFLINE_INSTALL.md`, and that the manifest still holds retired racing entries. Checked against the regenerated inventory: the first three **are** present and the retired `games/racing.js` entry is **gone** (R1's regeneration resolved 4 of the 5 claims as a side effect). Only the 4th remains, and on inspection it is *not* a bug — see below. So R2's remaining work is the validator + report, not another list fix.
    - **`docs/OFFLINE_INSTALL.md` is correctly excluded, and the roadmap's own exception covers it.** It lives at `game/docs/OFFLINE_INSTALL.md`, no runtime page loads it, and it is a caregiver document. It reaches users because `build_offline.js` builds the ZIP with `fs.cpSync(GAME, tmpDir, {recursive:true})` — i.e. the ZIP is the whole `game/` tree, a **superset** of `sw-cache-list.json`/`offline-manifest.json`, which are built from the list. That is the correct shape: the caregiver doc ships in the ZIP, and the runtime cache inventory stays free of docs-only files. Recorded as a classified exception rather than forced into runtime inventory.
    - **The ZIP/manifest divergence is a fact worth recording, not a bug:** manifest keys == cache-list keys (both derive from the list), while the ZIP additionally carries `game/docs/OFFLINE_INSTALL.md`. The validator reports the two inventories separately so this can never be mistaken for a mismatch.
    - **IMPLEMENTED: `tools/validate_offline.js` — 10 checks + the required report.** Every cache entry exists; no entry points at a retired file; manifest keys == cache-list keys exactly (modulo the self-exclusion below); manifest sha256 **and** size verified against the real bytes; **every local runtime dependency referenced by an HTML page is in the cache list** (260 refs across 18 pages — this is the check that would have caught the R1 `racing-config.js` bug at source); no `docs/`/`resources/` leakage; caregiver docs packaged-but-not-cached; **generation is deterministic** (re-runs `generate_sw_list.js` and requires byte-identical output); list is sorted. Report prints runtime pages/JS/CSS/images/audio/fonts/vendor, total files + bytes, and the 5 largest assets (biggest: `driving-car.png` 712 KB, `three.module.min.js` 671 KB, `dino/tiranosaurus-rex.png` 605 KB).
    - **BUG FOUND BY THE VALIDATOR ON ITS FIRST RUN — the offline manifest was hashing itself.** `offline-manifest.json` was listed inside its own contents. The manifest is written *after* the hashes are computed, so that entry could only ever hold the hash of the *previous* manifest. This was not cosmetic: `sw.js checkForUpdates()` diffs the online manifest against the cached one and reports every differing key, so `offline-manifest.json` was reported as **changed on every single update check** — meaning the parent area's «Проверити ажурирања» could never return a clean result. Fixed in `build_offline.js` by excluding the manifest from its own entries (it stays in `sw-cache-list.json`, because `sw.js` reads it back *out of the cache* to diff against), and the validator now **enforces** that exclusion so a self-entry can never reappear. Manifest is 206 entries against a 207-entry cache list.
    - **R2 acceptance chain passes end to end:** generate (207) → validate ✅ → build manifest (206) → validate ✅ → build ZIP (224 files) → validate ZIP ✅ (0 runtime entries missing; ZIP-only entries are directory entries plus the classified caregiver doc).
    - **SECOND BUG FOUND — `headless.js` leaked every Chrome process on Linux, and it is the likely real cause of the "load flakes" fixed earlier in R1.** `killChromeByTag()` only ever ran the **Windows `pwsh`** branch; on Linux/macOS that `execFileSync` throws, the `catch` swallows it, and `close()` reaped **nothing**. So every smoke run left its browser running. By the end of the session there were **21 orphaned Chromium processes** (10 from one finished `hub_smoke`, 7 from `touch_interruption`) still burning CPU and starving later runs. Fixed with a POSIX branch using `pkill -f user-data-dir=<tag>` (the per-run unique profile path makes it precise). Verified: a run now leaves **0** orphans (was 21). The impact was not subtle — `touch_interruption_smoke` went from **stuck on its first game for 8+ minutes** to **85/85 in 100 seconds** after the cleanup.
    - **Honest revision to R1's own record:** the four "parallel-load flakes" fixed earlier in this task (kitty / memory / puzzle / adventure) very likely included tests that were not flaky at all, but merely starved by this leak. The readiness-wait fixes are still correct and worth keeping — racing a navigation or a 900 ms animation is a genuine test defect — **but the environmental cause was ours, not the tests'.** Recorded so the next session does not read R1's flake narrative as four independent product-quality problems. On a machine without the leak, expect these to have been green already.
    - **Final validation after both fixes:** `node tools/run_all.js --concurrency 2 --resume /tmp/rr2` → **ALL 19 TOOLS PASS, 435 checks, 0 fail, 1 skip, 243.0 s of tool time (559 ms/check)**, slowest: `touch_interruption` 69.7 s, `piano` 25.5 s, `classroom` 18.0 s, `tracing` 12.9 s, `adventure` 12.1 s. All 19 `--resume` checkpoints written. The per-tool timing and `--- slowest ---` block are new in this task and are what made `touch_interruption`'s 22% share visible; optimising them is now **task 156**.
    - **⚠ MAJOR FINDING for R6 — `offline_smoke.mjs` does not actually test offline, and its 15 "passes" are largely false confidence.** Root-caused this session, not guessed: phase 1 and phase 2 call `start()` with **different tags**, and `start()` always creates a *unique* temp profile (`headless.js:125`), so **phase 2 starts with no service-worker registration and an empty Cache Storage**. It then calls `Network.setBlockedURLs({'*://*/*'})`, which also blocks `127.0.0.1`, so **no JavaScript can load at all** in phase 2 — only static markup is present. Proof: `index.html` reports `app.children === 3` and `animal_memory` renders its static difficulty buttons, but both are static HTML; the only two "failures" (`animal_counting`, `animal_puzzle`) are precisely the two pages that build their UI from JS at runtime (their `<div id="app">` is empty in markup and filled by `startAnimalCounting()`/`startAnimalPuzzle()`). So the test is really asserting "static markup exists", and the two red checks are the only ones that detected anything at all. A true offline test needs **one** browser session: register the SW, trigger `cacheAll`, visit the pages, then go offline in the *same* context so the SW can serve from Cache Storage. **This is R6's job (task 159) — deliberately not rewritten here.** Until then, treat the two red checks as the only signal that offline playability is untested, and do not read the 15 green ones as evidence.
    - **Also note:** those two pages' boot is `if (typeof startAnimalCounting === 'function') startAnimalCounting();` — a **silent** guard. If the game script fails to load, the page renders nothing with no error, which is exactly the failure mode seen here. Same family as the task-120 candy `showHint` no-op. Worth a look in R6.

- 166. DONE — R6: True Offline Play E2E. (2026-10-01, Ponytail Lazy Dev, user approved.)
    - **R2's finding was exact and is now closed.** `offline_smoke.mjs`'s two phases called `start()` with different tags, and `start()` always mints a unique profile, so phase 2 had **no service worker and an empty Cache Storage**; `Network.setBlockedURLs({'*://*/*'})` then blocked `127.0.0.1` too, so no JavaScript could load and the 15 green checks were asserting static markup only. **Rewritten as one session:** prime → cut the network → play, in a single browser context.
    - **The offline claim is now physically provable, not just emulated.** `headless.js` gained `closeServer()`, and phase 2 kills the origin process in addition to `Network.emulateNetworkConditions offline` + `Network.setCacheDisabled true`. Both controls are asserted: an uncached `?nocache=` fetch **must** come back BLOCKED (proves we are not merely flipping a flag), and a cached file **must** come back 200 from the SW (proves the cache works).
    - **Caching is awaited explicitly.** Phase 1 installs a `message` listener and waits for `cache-complete` (with live progress logged), after `navigator.serviceWorker.controller` is non-null. The old check was a **vacuous** `!!navigator.serviceWorker.ready` — always truthy, so it proved nothing. It now also requires `skipped: []` and compares Cache Storage against **all 207** `sw-cache-list.json` entries, and fails with the exact count on a shortfall.
    - **Coverage is asserted, not assumed.** The interaction table is keyed by registry ID and cross-checked against `app-registry.js`: 16 children ↔ 16 specs, exact match, so a new game cannot slip through untested.
    - **Every game is driven with trusted input and a geometry check.** `headless.js` gained CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` helpers (`tap`, `press`, `release`, `hold`, `drag`, `dragTo`, `stroke`, `boxOf`, `waitFor`). `boxOf` scrolls into view, requires the box to be inside the viewport, and samples 9 points through `elementFromPoint` so the tap lands on the intended element — the task-120 anti-pattern (`.click()` proving a control a child cannot reach) is structurally impossible now. The physics bug that makes this necessary: Chrome's `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` ignores `elementFromPoint`, so the old code resolved a hit-test target and then dispatched to a *different* point.
    - **Per-game coverage:** load+boot, a real primary interaction, an audio-failure call that must not throw, and back→hub navigation, for all 16 games, plus the hub (17 buttons), the group screen, the parent area's cached version, and «Проверити ажурирања» offline.
    - **3 REAL PRODUCT BUGS the honest test found** (all three are exactly the "unreachable control" family the geometry check exists for):
        - `game/pages/explorer.html` and `game/pages/parent.html` had `<button class="back-btn">` with **no `id`**, while `game/shared/main.js` wires `#back-btn` — the back button was dead on both pages.
        - `game/pages/racing3d.html`: `#r3d-back` had `z-index: 20` and the start modal `z-index: 25`, so **the start modal trapped the back button**. Raised to `30`.
        - `game/pages/parent.html`: offline, `sw.js checkForUpdates()`'s manifest fetch fails and the parent area's status stayed stuck at «Проверавам...» with the button disabled forever — i.e. a parent offline was told the app was still checking. Added a `check-error` handler → «Нема интернета — ажурирања се не могу проверити» + button re-enabled.
    - **A SKIP was hiding a real gap — found and removed.** The probe was `!!document.querySelector('#r3d-canvas')?.getContext ? ... : false`, i.e. it depended on a **runtime-built canvas id**. On a host with WebGL it silently reported "no WebGL" and downgraded the race to a SKIP — a check that could not fail, the same family as R5's findings. Fixed to probe a throwaway canvas exactly as `racing3d_smoke.js` does. Consequence: **racing3d now has real offline coverage** (countdown → steer right → release) and the run finishes with **0 skips**. The readiness wait was also wrong twice over and both were fixed honestly: the start button exists in markup before the modal is shown (so wait on `rect.width > 4`), and `lateral` does **not** auto-centre, it only clamps to `MAX_LATERAL` — assert the clamp, do not invent a centre-on-release behaviour the game never had.
    - **`matching_game` also needed a real fix, not a loosened assertion.** The hint button can legitimately answer «Нема потеза — сачекај звезду ⭐»; the game then spawns a star 400 ms later (`candy.js:157`) and tapping it refills the cell (`explodeStarAt`). The test now **drives that path** instead of asserting on a hint the game honestly refused to give. (My first attempt at a "playable board" readiness wait was itself vacuous — no `.hint-float` exists yet, so the condition held before any hint. Deleted.)
    - **Promoted to blocking, as the roadmap requires.** `check_release.js` runs it as a real stage (only `axe_check --report` stays advisory, per R10), and `ci.yml`'s `release` job lost `continue-on-error`. It is deliberately **not** in the 24-tool battery — it owns a profile and a server for minutes.
    - **Validation:** `check_fast.js` **6/6 green**; `offline_smoke.mjs` **101 checks, 0 fail, 0 skip**, ending `PASS acceptance: every offline game loads, plays and returns (16 games) [all green]`, reproduced on consecutive runs; `racing3d_smoke.js` 32/32; `parent_smoke.js` and `kitty_smoke.js` green. `docs/` synced (208 files) and the canonical 206-entry manifest restored after the Windows `.ps1` fallback wrote a self-including 207-entry one.
    - **Next: R7** (per the roadmap table in `HANDOVER_PROMPT.md`).

- 165. DONE — R5: Make Validation Commands Semantically Clean. (2026-09-30, Ponytail Lazy Dev, user approved.)
    - **Goal (roadmap R5, P0, depends on R4):** "Stop having commands with overlapping or misleading meanings." A release check must **fail when generated artifacts are out of date rather than silently rewriting them.**
    - **Delivered, one meaning per command:** `check_syntax.js` read-only (R4) · `check_fast.js` read-only gate (NEW) · `run_all.js` targeted runner · `check_release.js` read-only release gate (REWRITTEN) · `sync-docs.sh` + `generate_sw_list.js` + `build_offline.js` explicit generation · `validate_generated.js` freshness comparison (NEW) · `validate_workflow.js` CI-topology guard (NEW).
    - **NEW `tools/check_fast.js`** — 6 read-only stages: `check_syntax.js`, `validate_pages.js` (registry/metadata), `validate_workflow.js`, `validate_generated.js`, `validate_offline.js`, `hub_smoke.js`. Green in ~9 s. It **retries a stage that exits non-zero with 0 checks** (the documented Chrome-boot-crash signature) up to 2 times, because on this host a 0-check crash is a resource spike, not a failed assertion — and the first real run hit exactly that.
    - **NEW `tools/validate_generated.js`** — compares the committed `docs/` mirror against `game/` (**208 files**: same names, canonically identical bytes, neither side holding an extra file) and checks `game/sw-cache-list.json` against `generate_sw_list.js --stdout` (**207 entries**). Every failure names the fix (`sync-docs.sh` / `generate_sw_list.js`) instead of repairing the file. Negative-tested: a `docs/index.html` drift, a moved manifest and a stale cache list are each caught.
    - **NEW `tools/validate_workflow.js`** — 11 checks over `ci.yml`: every `tools/*.js` it invokes exists; every `needs.<job>.outputs` read has a **direct** `needs` entry (the task-164 silent-failure mode, now permanent); no tabs; no CR; balanced `${{ }}`; every job declares `runs-on`; every step has `uses:`/`run:`; matrix comes from `needs.setup.outputs`; `fail-fast: false`; setup derives the battery from `run_all --list --json`; `extended` is manual/schedule only. Negative-tested — 5 deliberate mutations (deleting a referenced tool, indirect `needs`, removing `fail-fast`, hand-listing the matrix, un-gating `extended`) each fail.
    - **REWRITTEN `tools/check_release.js`** — was three commands wearing one name: its "syntax: node --check all files" step called `check_all.js` (**the entire 24-tool battery**), it ran `sync-docs.sh` (rewriting all of `docs/`), and in release mode it ran `build_offline.js` (regenerating the manifest + ZIP). Now: `check_fast` + the battery **through `run_all.js`** (keeping its parallelism, boot-crash retries and `--resume`, which the old hand-rolled one-process-per-smoke loop threw away) + two **advisory** stages (`offline_smoke.mjs` known-red until R6, `axe_check.js --report` informational until R10) that print their real exit code and are labelled `ADVISORY`, never dressed up as a pass. `--fast` skips the battery, `--concurrency/--resume/--game/--since` pass through.
    - **The read-only contract is ENFORCED, not just documented:** `check_release.js` fingerprints `docs/` (recursive), `game/sw-cache-list.json`, `game/offline-manifest.json` and `docs/game-offline.zip` before and after, and exits non-zero listing whatever changed. Negative-tested: the fingerprint distinguishes a single appended byte, and a drifted `docs/index.html` makes the gate fail rather than repair.
    - **BUG 1 — `validate_offline.js` was not read-only, and its determinism check was vacuous.** It re-ran the generator in **write** mode and then compared the file with itself (`before` vs `after` — both read *after* the generator had already overwritten it). So: a "validator" mutated `game/sw-cache-list.json` on every run; a **stale cache list was silently repaired instead of reported**, which is the exact anti-pattern R5 exists to kill; and the LF rewrite dirtied the worktree with a phantom ` M game/sw-cache-list.json` on **every** run on this CRLF checkout. Now invokes `generate_sw_list.js --stdout` and compares canonical buffers — proven: mtime unchanged, and an injected stale entry is **reported** with `run: node tools/generate_sw_list.js`.
    - **BUG 2 — `build_offline.js` destroyed the artifact it was replacing.** It did `if (exists) unlink(zipPath)` *before* `zip -r`, so any failure after that point left no archive at all — and the very next line fails on this host because `zip` is not on PATH. Now builds to `game-offline.zip.tmp` and `renameSync`s on success, cleaning the temp on any error and printing the actionable cause (including the `.ps1` fallback). Negative-tested with a stand-in archive: 64 bytes before, 64 bytes after the failed build, no `.tmp` residue. `execSync` import dropped for `execFileSync` (no shell-interpolated path).
    - **CI `fast` is now one step** — `node tools/check_fast.js` — so the gate has a single definition instead of four steps duplicated in YAML; `release` no longer re-runs `validate_offline` (already in the fast gate) and keeps the two advisory steps. `check_release.js` is still deliberately **not** in CI: it re-runs the whole battery the pipeline already ran in parallel, one leg per job.
    - **NEW `.gitattributes`** pinning `.github/workflows/*.yml` to `eol=lf`. `validate_workflow` asserts no CR (a `\r` in a `run:` block breaks the shell), but `core.autocrlf=true` would check the file out as CRLF on a fresh Windows clone — a machine-dependent gate, the same class task 164 just removed from the manifest. `game/`+`docs/` are intentionally **not** pinned; they stay canonical-compared.
    - **BUG 3 — `hub_smoke.js` raced page load, so the fast gate was ~20% red for the wrong reason.** Making `check_fast` the CI gate surfaced a flake nobody had chased: the games-tile check failed intermittently with `active:"hub", go:""` while the learning-tile check (run a moment later) always passed. It is **not** the app and **not** a slow swap. Instrumented proof: on failing runs `goTo` was **never called** (`window.__trace` empty) and `window.goTo` was still `undefined` at click time — the click landed **before `shared/navigation.js` had executed**, so the tile had no listener and the event was simply dropped. The old readiness wait only polled `document.getElementById('hub').classList.contains('active')`, which is true in the **shipped markup** — it proved the HTML parsed, never that any script had run. Fixed with a readiness wait on script execution (`typeof window.goTo === 'function'`) plus post-click waits that require the *assertion-grade* state (active screen **and** 10 wired `data-go` buttons), and the games-tile title probe is now scoped to the active panel instead of `document.querySelector('.hub-sub-title')`, which always matched the first sub-title in the DOM and masked which panel was live. Negative-tested both ways: **28/28 runs green, 0 fail** (2 boot crashes = the known host resource spike), and unwiring one games button still fails 2 checks with `ready:false`. A readiness wait, not a longer sleep and not a loosened assertion — and `game/` was not bent to suit the test.
    - **Validation:** `check_fast.js` 6/6 stages green (~9 s); `check_release.js --fast` green and self-verified read-only; `validate_workflow.js` 11/11; `validate_generated.js` 4/4; `validate_offline.js` green with the cache list provably untouched; `hub_smoke.js` 28/28. Full 24-tool battery **not** run (R5 touches only `tools/` + CI + docs, and this host is killed by resource spikes). Not committed — user commits/pushes.
- 164. DONE — R4: CI Topology Refactor. (2026-09-30, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - **CI WAS RED BEFORE THIS TASK — verified, not assumed.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:79` ran `node tools/touch_interruption_smoke.js`, a file **deleted** in `e4817a4` (task 156 split the monolith into 4 shards), so the `release` job failed on every push to `main` — and touch-interruption coverage vanished from CI with it. Two more topology faults: the `fast` job ran `node tools/check_all.js`, which runs the **entire 24-tool battery** (the exact opposite of R4's Job A, and the slowest job in the workflow), and the hand-listed 16-smoke matrix was already stale — `adventure_smoke`, `parent_smoke`, `sw_update_smoke` and all four touch shards never ran. No `fail-fast: false`, so one failure cancelled every in-flight leg.
    - **Workflow rewritten to R4's four layers + a setup job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`):**
        - `setup` — generates the smoke matrix **from the battery** via `run_all.js --list --json`, minus `hub_smoke.js`. 24 tools = 1 hub leg + **23 matrix legs**. A newly added smoke can no longer be silently left out of CI, which is the staleness class that had already bitten this repo's documented commands three times.
        - `fast` (Job A) — `check_syntax.js` → `validate_pages.js` (registry/routes + PWA metadata) → `validate_offline.js` (deployment artifacts) → `hub_smoke.js`. **No game battery in this job**, which is what R4 requires.
        - `smoke` (Job B) — one leg per smoke, **`fail-fast: false`** (R4's key rule: one game must never hide the others), `max-parallel: 6`, `timeout-minutes: 10`.
        - `release` (Job C) — `needs: [fast, smoke]`; offline-inventory validation, offline E2E and the a11y report. It deliberately does **not** call `check_release.js`: that script is still *mutating* (it rewrites `docs/` via `sync-docs.sh`, rebuilds the offline pack, and uses `check_all.js` for its "syntax" step, which re-runs the whole battery). R5 makes it read-only; only then does it belong here.
        - `extended` (Job D) — `play_matrix.mjs` (chromium + webkit × 5 viewports), manual `workflow_dispatch` or weekly only, never on every push.
        - Also added `permissions: contents: read`, `workflow_dispatch`, and a weekly `schedule` so the expensive layer still runs unattended.
    - **Two of the three release-job steps are `continue-on-error`, deliberately and honestly:** `offline_smoke.mjs` is **KNOWN RED** (R2/task-153 finding: its phase 2 starts a fresh profile with no service worker and then blocks `127.0.0.1` too, so it only verifies static markup — R6 rebuilds it), and `axe_check.js --report` fetches axe-core from a CDN and only becomes a blocking gate in R10. Shipping either as a required check would have kept CI red for a documented reason; both still run so their output stays visible.
    - **New `tools/check_syntax.js`** — read-only `node --check` over `game/` + `tools/`, exporting `runSyntax()`. Extracted because the only syntax gate was `check_all.js`, which also runs the battery, so Job A could not check syntax without paying for everything. `check_all.js` now `require`s it and its duplicate implementation is deleted — one implementation, no behaviour change (its `Syntax OK.` output is unchanged).
    - **`tools/run_all.js` gained `--json`** (with `--list`) → prints the battery as a JSON array. Mirrors `validate_pages.js --json`; it is the mechanism that makes the CI matrix self-maintaining.
    - **TWO REAL BUGS found while making Job A's offline gate trustworthy — both fixed at the root:**
        - **(a) `build_offline.js` crashed on this repo's own path.** Line 18 built the generator command by string interpolation: `execSync('node ' + path.join(TOOLS, 'generate_sw_list.js'))`. The path is unquoted, and this repo lives at `E:\GitHub\Games for kids`, so the build died with `Cannot find module 'E:\GitHub\Games'` **before generating anything**. Fixed with `execFileSync(process.execPath, [script])` — argv array, no shell, so no quoting class of bug is possible. Same family as task 148's `screenshot.js`, task 150's `axe_check.js` and task 152's `--concurrency 6`: **a documented entry point that silently does not work.**
        - **(b) The offline manifest hashed raw work-tree bytes, so it depended on the machine that built it.** There is no `.gitattributes` and this Windows checkout has `core.autocrlf=true`, so text files are CRLF on disk while git — and therefore GitHub Pages, and therefore any CI checkout — has LF. Measured on the committed manifest: of 63 text entries, **13 were hashed as CRLF and 50 as LF**, i.e. a mixed manifest. It happens to verify today (all 206 entries match canonically), so CI was *not* red from this — **but the next rebuild on this machine would have baked CRLF hashes in**, after which every Linux checkout and every parent's «Проверити ажурирања» would report the whole app as changed. Fixed by hashing **canonical** bytes through the new shared `tools/manifest_hash.js` (text → LF, binary with a NUL byte → identity), used by BOTH `build_offline.js` and `validate_offline.js` so builder and validator can never disagree about what a hash means. `validate_offline.js`'s "generation is deterministic" check now compares canonically too: comparing raw bytes made it report the list as changed on Windows only (CRLF worktree copy vs the LF file the generator rewrites), i.e. a gate that would have been permanently red locally while green in CI.
        - **Proof the hashing change does not alter shipped bytes:** `node tools/build_offline.js` regenerates `game/offline-manifest.json` (206 entries) and `game/sw-cache-list.json` (207) **byte-for-byte identical to the committed files**. So there is **no `game/` change, no `docs/` re-sync and no offline-pack rebuild** in this task — the worktree after validation contains only `tools/` + workflow edits.
    - **Validation (deliberately scoped — AGENTS.md: smallest relevant command, and this host kills long runs):** `node tools/check_syntax.js` → 84 files OK; `validate_pages.js` → 17 pages OK; **`validate_offline.js` → 10/10 PASS (it was 3 checks failing before the hashing fix)**; `hub_smoke.js` → ALL PASS; `check_all.js --list` → the refactored syntax stage + runner hand-off still exit 0; `node --check` clean. No smoke was re-run because no smoke and no `game/` file changed (verified: no smoke requires any of the changed tools).
    - **CI cannot be executed here, so the workflow was structurally probed with a throwaway script (deleted after use):** no tabs, 5 jobs all declaring `runs-on`, every step carrying `uses:`/`run:`, `fail-fast: false` present, every `needs.X.outputs` reference satisfied by a direct `needs`, and **every `node tools/...` target in the file exists on disk**. **Every check was negative-tested, because a probe that cannot fail proves nothing:** re-inserting the exact line that broke CI (`run: node tools/touch_interruption_smoke.js`) made it report `missing tool file: tools/touch_interruption_smoke.js`, and reverting `needs: [setup, fast]` to `needs: fast` made it report `job smoke reads needs.setup.outputs but its direct needs are [fast]`. The `setup` job's matrix command was also run **verbatim** locally with `GITHUB_OUTPUT` pointed at a temp file → `battery: 24 tools -> 23 smoke legs`. **The definitive proof is the real CI run, which can only happen after the user commits and pushes — not claimed here.** (Process note, recorded because it nearly caused a real mistake: the first negative test was run inline as `node -e "…"` inside PowerShell, where `$` interpolation and my own `-replace … | Out-Null` "restore" — which writes nothing — left the workflow file sitting in the deliberately-broken state. Caught by re-reading the file instead of trusting the output, and redone via a temp script file.)
    - **THIRD REAL BUG, caught by a self-check before it could ship: the `smoke` job read `needs.setup.outputs.smokes` while declaring only `needs: fast`.** The `needs` context exposes **direct** dependencies only, so `needs.setup` would have been empty, `fromJSON('')` would have thrown, and **the entire 23-leg smoke layer would have silently never run** — a green-looking pipeline with zero game coverage, which is the exact failure mode R4 exists to prevent. Fixed to `needs: [setup, fast]`, with the reason in a comment so the next editor does not "simplify" it back.
    - **Gaps found and deliberately left for R5 (command semantics), not fixed here:** `check_release.js` is still mutating (above); `node tools/build_offline.js` **cannot complete on this machine** because the ZIP step shells out to `zip`, which is not installed (`tools/build_offline.ps1` + `Compress-Archive` remain the Windows fallback) — so `check_all.js --offline` cannot complete here either, and the ZIP step also unlinks the old archive before it can create the new one, which is a destructive-failure hazard that belongs with the `zip` fix. **Idea for R5, now justified twice over:** promote the throwaway structural probe into a permanent `tools/validate_workflow.js` with two checks — (1) every `node tools/...` target in `ci.yml` exists, and (2) every `needs.X.outputs` reference in a job is satisfied by that job's **direct** `needs`. Both are ~10 lines each, both have been negative-tested here, and each one independently catches a way this workflow could report success while testing nothing.

- 156. DONE — Test suite resource budget & speed (2026-10-02, Ponytail Lazy Dev; completed before R16 per user direction). **The host kills the process on a usage spike.**
    - **User's diagnosis, accepted and recorded:** opencode runs inside the **Acode app on a phone**, and that sandbox appears to enforce CPU/RAM failsafe limits that **kill the process outright on a usage spike**. This explains the repeated mid-run restarts that plagued R1/R2 (multiple `check_all` and `run_all` invocations died with no output, taking unfinished work with them). **Design consequence: never rely on one long run completing. Runs must be short, low-concurrency, and resumable.** Do not "fix" a killed run by re-running the whole battery.
    - **Measured baseline (2026-09-30, concurrency 4, after the Chrome-leak fix): 287.3 s of tool time across 19 tools / 435 checks = 660 ms/check.** Slowest: `touch_interruption` 64.0 s (85 checks), `piano` 28.4 s (22 checks, 1290 ms/check — worst ratio), `tracing` 16.9 s, `classroom` 16.9 s, `adventure` 16.8 s. Fastest: `coloring` 7.3 s, `racing3d` 7.7 s. Before the leak fix, `touch_interruption` alone hung **8+ minutes on its first game** instead of 64 s.
    - **Where the time goes (measured, to be confirmed before optimising):** (a) **fixed Chrome boot per smoke** — the fastest tool still takes 7.3 s for 21 checks, and 19 tools × roughly that floor is a large share of the total; (b) **`touch_interruption` opens 17 games** and is 22% of total time on its own — the single biggest target, and the likely fix is **one Chrome reused across all 17 games** instead of one per game; (c) **fixed `sleep()` between steps** — 660 ms/check average means most of the cost is waiting, not asserting, so sleeps are the thing to prune (carefully: AGENTS.md forbids trading a readiness wait for a shorter sleep).
    - **Already landed as the immediate resilience work:** `run_all.js` gained per-tool timing + a `--- slowest ---` block and ms/check total in its summary (**measure before optimising**), a `--sequential` flag, `$RUN_ALL_CONCURRENCY` to lower the worker count without editing code, and **`--resume [dir]`**, which writes a checkpoint per smoke as it lands and skips smokes that already passed. Measured: a repeated run drops from 11.8 s of tool time to 0.29 s. Default checkpoint dir is `$TMPDIR/run_all_resume`.
    - **TOUCH INTERRUPTION SPLIT (done).** The old `touch_interruption_smoke.js` was one 85-check tool that opened all 17 games in a single process — ~22% of total wall time, and one host restart lost all of it. Now `tools/touch_interruption_core.js` holds the shared per-game logic, and four 5-line entry points `touch_interruption_{a,b,c,d}_smoke.js` slice the registry into **4/4/4/5** games. `run_all` schedules them as independent tools (battery is now **23 tools**) and `--resume` checkpoints each one, so a kill costs one shard. Coverage is provably identical: verified **17/17 games covered, 0 missing, 0 duplicated**; every shard is `games × 5 checks`. `tools/touch_interruption_all.js` runs all four in one process for a quick local pass but is deliberately **not** auto-included (its name does not end in `_smoke.js`). New timings: the slowest tools are now `touch_interruption_a/c/d` at 26–28 s each instead of one 69.7 s monolith — the parallel scheduler hides the split, and the whole battery runs in bounded chunks. **`piano` (25.2 s) is now the single slowest non-shard tool** and is the next optimisation target.
    - **Note on shard balance:** shards are ordered contiguous slices with proportional bounds (not `ceil`-chunks, which would leave the last shard nearly empty at 5/5/5/2).
    - **Initial next steps (completed below):** profile `piano` and prune provably-unnecessary sleeps across the suite. **Every optimisation must keep the suite honest** — no check may be deleted, weakened, or converted to a skip to buy speed. Re-measure and record the before/after table here.
    - **PROFILED 2026-10-02 (Ponytail Lazy Dev).** Fresh baseline, `--concurrency 2 --resume`: **260.1 s across 27 tools / 570 checks = 456 ms/check**. Slowest: `piano` 35.3 s, `racing3d` 27.0 s, `touch_interruption_d` 24.5 s, `touch_interruption_a/b` 16.3/16.2 s. `piano` in isolation: **22.3 s / 25 checks = 892 ms/check**.
    - **Piano profiled by timestamping every console line** (`node -r <preload> tools/piano_smoke.js`, so the smoke itself is untouched): boot → first check 814 ms; main body → 1690 ms; **the 42-tap loop 1690 → 13008 ms = 11.3 s (269 ms per tap)**; replay 94 ms; **teardown + static registry checks 13164 → 15609 = 2.45 s**; reduced-motion check 0.76 s; 390 px geometry 16372 → 18551 = 2.18 s.
    - **Finding that overturns hypothesis (c) for piano: its taps are tempo-bound, not sleep-bound.** The explicit sleeps in `piano_smoke.js` total only ~1.3 s of the 22.3 s (6%), and the dominant `sleep(330)` inside the 42-tap loop was largely honest — the *game* needs ~270 ms to consume a note and advance. Replacing that fixed sleep with a **readiness wait** on the real condition (the counter changes, or the finish panel appears) cut it 342 → **269 ms per tap**, taking `piano` from 22.3 s to **20.5 s**. The change is kept because it is both faster and stricter — a fixed sleep "passes by hoping 330 ms was enough", the wait fails within 5 s if the game does not advance — but it is ~1.8 s, not the ~12 s the raw profile suggested. **Do not expect sleep-pruning to pay here; the game is doing the work.**
    - **Previously identified target (completed below): fixed per-smoke teardown.** `close()` costs ~2.45 s per smoke (kill-by-tag + the 20 × 50 ms teardown poll + `fs.rmSync` of a ~20 MB profile), so ~66 s of the 260 s battery — about **25%**, and it is paid once per tool regardless of how few checks the tool has. That is the large half of hypothesis (a) ("fixed cost per smoke"), and unlike sleep-pruning it is pure overhead with no assertion attached. **Completed and measured below.**
    - **Teardown profiled and optimized (2026-10-02).** Before: 3 instrumented close runs took 4365 / 2448 / 2116 ms; median **2448 ms**, of which the Windows `pwsh` process-tag kill alone took 2080–4328 ms. The profile-directory removal cost only ~32 ms; the 20×50 ms poll was not the bottleneck. `headless.js` now uses the exact `ChildProcess` handle already returned by `execFile` on Windows, waits for the debug port to close, and falls back to the existing exact-profile process match if it does not. POSIX keeps its existing profile-tag kill path. A direct-kill probe verified the debug port closed and **zero tagged Chrome processes remained**. After: five instrumented closes took 50–55 ms (**52 ms average**, ~97.9% below the prior median); the PowerShell spawn is absent on the normal path.
    - **Re-measurement (2026-10-02, concurrency 2, resumable fresh checkpoint):** all **27/27 tools passed, 575 checks, 140.4 s aggregate tool time (244 ms/check)**. The previous recorded run was 260.1 s / 570 checks under concurrency 2; timing across runs is host/load-sensitive, so the isolated close measurements—not the suite delta—attribute the improvement to the change. All four touch-interruption shards passed, exercising repeated Chrome close/reboot behavior. `node tools/check_fast.js` passed 7/7; the CDP disconnect negative test passed 7/7; `node --check` and `git diff --check` were clean.
    - **Conclusion:** task-156 resilience, instrumentation, touch-shard split, piano readiness wait, and teardown profiling/optimization are complete. No assertion was removed, weakened, or skipped. R16 remains unstarted as requested.
    - **Operating rule for constrained hosts:** `node tools/run_all.js --concurrency 2 --resume` (or `--sequential` for the heaviest tools like `touch_interruption`), and check for orphaned Chromium processes first if anything is unexpectedly slow.

- 154. DONE — R3: Service Worker and Update Path Correctness. (2026-09-30, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - **LIVE PRODUCTION BUG, not a latent one.** `sw.js:144` read the cached offline manifest with the hard-coded path `'/game/offline-manifest.json'`, while the network fetch on the line above already used the scope-derived `OFFLINE_MANIFEST_URL = new URL('offline-manifest.json', APP_ROOT)`. This project is served by **GitHub Pages from `docs/`**, i.e. from a subpath (`https://radmanmilos.github.io/Games-for-kids/`), so the real cache key has never been under `/game/`. Consequences, all user-visible on the published site: `cache.match()` missed → `cachedManifest` stayed `null` → **every single file** was reported as changed on **every** update check. Combined with the manifest self-hash bug fixed in task 153, the parent area's «Проверити ажурирања» was doubly incapable of ever reporting "up to date". Fixed by using the same `OFFLINE_MANIFEST_URL` for both the network fetch and the cache lookup, so there is no duplicated path string. It was the only hard-coded `/game/` path left in `sw.js`.
    - **Added `tools/sw_update_smoke.js` (8 checks), served under a deliberate subpath `/some/deep/prefix/`** so any future deployment-path assumption fails immediately: worker registers and controls the page; `scriptURL` proves the scope follows the subpath; the manifest is in the cache under its **scope-relative** URL; **no server change → `changed: 0`**; a changed file is detected **and named**; a **removed** file is still reported (union diff); and re-caching an identical manifest returns to `changed: 0`.
    - **Negative-tested, which matters more than the green run:** reverting the one-line fix makes **5 of the 8** checks fail with the exact production symptom — `changed: 4, changes: [every file]` on every call. The test genuinely pins the bug rather than passing vacuously.
    - **Battery is now 20 tools** (the runner auto-includes `*_smoke.js`), so this is enforced on every run.
    - **Still not covered:** a true offline test. `offline_smoke.mjs` cannot verify playability (see task 155 — phase 2 has no SW and no cache); making it behavioral is **R6 / task 159**.

- 163. DONE — R12: Remove Technical Controls from the Child Hub. (2026-09-30, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - **Child side was already done in task 155** (the download button, status line, update-check button and ZIP link were removed from `index.html` when the user reported them). R12's remaining work was entirely on the **parent** side, plus the acceptance guard.
    - **Built on the parent surface (R12's required list):** a live **connection status** (Онлајн / Офлајн, driven by `navigator.onLine` + `online`/`offline` events, with a red/green dot), an app **version**, and the **manual ZIP link** — the three items that were missing. Existing download, update check, reset progress and audio test were already there.
    - **Version has a single source of truth:** added `"version": "2.0.0"` to `game/manifest.json`; the parent page fetches it (the manifest is in the cache list, so it still resolves offline). Deliberately **not** derived from `sw.js`'s `CACHE_NAME`, which would have created a second copy of the same fact.
    - **New `tools/parent_smoke.js` (6 checks)** — parent surface has the four actions; ZIP link present with the `download` attribute and the right href; version resolves to a real `x.y.z`; live connection status; back button; audio test wired.
    - **New hub guards (R12's stated acceptance: "Hub smoke must be updated to assert this")** — two static checks on `index.html`: no technical/offline control id may appear on the child launcher, and there must be exactly one `data-go="game-parent"` entry point. **Negative-tested**: injecting a `#download-offline` button into the hub makes the guard fail with `leaked: download-offline`, and it passes again on restore.
    - **A real bug of mine, caught by the parallel battery:** the first `parent_smoke` **passed solo and failed under load** because its version-polling loop called `JSON.parse` on a string that `evalv` had already returned — the code path only ran when the manifest fetch was slow. Fixed and rewritten as a proper readiness wait. Battery: **ALL 24 TOOLS PASS.**
    - **⚠ Manual ZIP link — RESOLVED by user decision (2026-09-30): HIDDEN, not deleted.** R12 asks the parent surface to offer a manual ZIP, but `build_offline.js` writes the archive to `docs/game-offline.zip` and that file has been **untracked since `83aae92`**, so GitHub Pages never publishes it and the link would 404 for a real parent. The user chose to **hide the option for now and revisit it later**, rather than re-track a ~6.4 MB generated binary. Implementation: the anchor stays in the markup carrying `hidden` (so restoring it is one attribute), plus an explicit `[hidden]{display:none !important}` rule — needed because `.parent-link{display:block}` is an author rule and would otherwise beat the UA stylesheet's `[hidden]`. `parent_smoke.js` now asserts the link is **present but not visible** (`visible === false && hiddenAttr === true`), so the decision is pinned by a test and cannot silently regress into a dead link. **To revisit: publish the ZIP (track it, or host it as a release asset), then drop the `hidden` attribute and flip that check.**

    - **Validation (deliberately scoped):** `node tools/parent_smoke.js` → **6/6 PASS** — the only tool that covers this change. The full 24-tool battery was **not** used to validate a one-line attribute change (see the AGENTS.md note added in task 163: it is slow on this host and its red entries were the tools I terminated, not assertion failures). `docs/` synced, offline rebuilt (206-entry manifest), `validate_offline.js` PASS, `node --check` clean.
- 155. BUGFIX — user-reported, found during the R1 session (2026-09-30, Ponytail Lazy Dev.)
    - **"Мала истраживачица" (explorer) would not start — unplayable for a child.** `#char-modal` in `game/pages/explorer.html` had **no CSS rule at all**, so it stayed in normal flow inside `#app` (`position:fixed; inset:0; overflow:hidden`) and its two hero buttons rendered at viewport **y≈1093–1149 in a 700px viewport** — clipped off-screen and untappable. The child saw the HUD and the "Изабери лик" heading but could not tap a hero, so the game never started. Its sibling modals `#win-modal` / `#worlds-modal` both had proper `position:absolute;inset:0;z-index:20` + `.show{display:flex}` rules; `#char-modal` was simply missed. Fixed by giving it the same modal treatment.
    - **Why the smoke missed it (the important part):** `kitty_smoke` starts the game with `document.querySelector('.char-btn').click()`, which **bypasses hit-testing entirely** — so it passed while the real control was unreachable. This is the task-120 anti-pattern again in a new shape: not a stub, but a test exercising a code path a child cannot use. Added a regression guard asserting both hero buttons are **fully inside the viewport and are the topmost element at their own centre** (`elementFromPoint`). `kitty_smoke` is now 28/28.
    - **Parent-area duplication: the download buttons were still on the child-facing main screen.** Task 147 added `pages/parent.html` behind the 🔒 lock, but `index.html` kept its own copy of the parent controls — a 140×140 offline-download button, a status line, a "Проверити ажурирања" button and a "ZIP за ручно преузимање" link. Removed from the hub; they now exist only in the parent area. `navigation.js` already guarded the old ids with `if (!btn) return`, so nothing broke, and `tools/offline_smoke.mjs` phase 1 now opens `/pages/parent.html` (the page that actually has `#download-offline`) so its explicit `cacheAll` trigger still fires instead of silently no-opping. `hub_smoke` 10/10; `offline_smoke` phase 1 now caches all 17 pages. **Note: `offline_smoke` still reports 2 failures — `animal_counting` and `animal_puzzle` render no UI offline** (their `<div id="app">` is empty in markup and the game builds into it at runtime). Their scripts, page and data files are all present in `sw-cache-list.json`, so this is NOT a missing-cache-entry problem; it is a service-worker path/runtime issue and belongs to **R2 (offline inventory) and R3 (service-worker/update path correctness)**. Pre-existing, not caused by this task, and deliberately left for R2/R3 rather than half-fixed here.
    - **Lesson to carry forward: a passing smoke proves the assertions ran, not that a child can reach the control.** Prefer real pointer dispatch plus geometry/hit-testing assertions over `.click()` wherever a test stands in for a human interaction.

- 120. DONE — Phase 2, Game 7: GAME-CLASS-001 — Calm classroom hub. (2026-09-27, Ponytail Lazy Dev; user approved.)
    - **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-27.** Added visual category tabs (Азбука/Бројеви/Облици/Боје) to classroom.js/classroom.html. Tabs show active state, switch between activities without going back to hub. `kids_smoke.js` renamed to `classroom_smoke.js` + tab checks added. Exposed `isBusy()` on `window.kidsGame` for test synchronization.
    - **ROOT-CAUSE FIX 2026-09-27 — the "8 corrects -> finish panel" failure was a real product bug, not a test-sync problem.** A throwaway probe (`tools/debug_class_finish_probe.js`, deleted after use) captured the page errors and showed the last click threw `TypeError: Failed to execute 'setValueAtTime' on 'AudioParam': The provided double value is non-finite` and `busy` stayed stuck true forever, so every later click was ignored. Root cause: `window.tone`'s real signature is `(freq, dur, delay, type, vol)` (`shared/audio.js:29`), but **both** shared feedback modules passed the oscillator `type` in the `delay` slot — `shared/feedback.js` (4 call sites) and `shared/audio-buses.js` (`busTone`, 1 call site) — so `start = currentTime + 'sine'` = `NaN` and `setValueAtTime` threw. `shared/celebration.js:20` calls `window.successChime()` **first**, so the throw aborted the rest of `finish()` in `kids_games.js:113` — the 🏅 finish panel and the "N од 8 тачно!" score never appeared for a child who completed any "За децу" quiz. The same throw silently killed the wrong-answer path too (`kids_games.js:106` calls `gentleMiss()` before adding `.wrong` / setting "Покушај још једном!"), so a wrong tap did nothing visible. Both were broken on all **13 pages** that load the shared feedback modules (task 110 FB-001 / task 113 AUDIO-001 shipped with the wrong signature). Fixed at the source: all 5 call sites now pass the delay explicitly.
    - **Test hardening (so this class of bug cannot hide again):** `classroom_smoke.js` used to stub the *wrappers* `window.popSound`/`window.gentleMiss`, which masked the broken shared feedback path. It now stubs only the *primitives* (`window.tone`, `window.sweep`, `window.speech`), so the real `popSound`/`gentleMiss`/`successChime`/`celebrate` code runs in headless — a signature regression now throws inside the click handler and the existing finish-panel + wrong-tap checks fail. Also fixed the stale `node tools/kids_smoke.js` run line in the file header.
    - **Validation:** `node tools/classroom_smoke.js` → **17/17 ALL PASS** (was 16 pass / 1 fail). `node tools/check_all.js --docs --offline`: syntax OK, **19/19 tools green, 398 checks, 0 fail**; `docs/` re-synced (209 files); offline pack rebuilt (`sw-cache-list.json` 208 entries, `offline-manifest.json` 208 entries, `docs/game-offline.zip`). NOT committed (user commits/pushes).
    - **SECOND FIX, same session (2026-09-27) — the candy 💡 hint button had been dead since task 110 shipped.** `candy_smoke.js` failed 2 checks with `{"msg":"","hinted":0}`. Reproducible in isolation and identical on the committed tree (`git stash`-verified), so it was a real bug, not the "environment flake" it had been recorded as. A throwaway probe (`tools/debug_candy_hint_probe.js`, deleted after use) showed `findHint()` returning a valid swap (`{a:[0,0],b:[1,0],type:1}`) and `showHint()` returning **silently, with zero errors and zero side effects**. Root cause: a **global name collision** — `candy.js` declared `function showHint()` at top level (a classic script, so it becomes `window.showHint`), and `shared/feedback.js` exports its own `window.showHint(el)` (adds the `.ps-hint` class to an element). `matching_game.html` loads `games/candy.js` at line 282 and `shared/feedback.js` at line **286**, so the shared module overwrote the game's function; the button's handler then called the shared version with `el === undefined`, which hits `if (!el) return;` and does nothing. Fixed by renaming the game-local function to `showCandyHint()` (definition + the one call site) — the collision-free side, so the shared API is untouched. `candy_smoke.js` **12/12 ALL PASS** (`{"msg":"Погледај Слонови 🐘 😉","hinted":2}`). The 2 existing hint checks now pin this regression.
    - **Lesson worth keeping:** a shared module that loads *after* a game script can silently replace any same-named top-level function. `showHint` was the only such collision today (checked all 6 names `feedback.js` exports), but the pattern is a landmine — recorded in `AGENTS.md` → Footguns.

- 121. DONE — Phase 2, Game 8: GAME-PIANO-001 — Free-play-first piano ("Прати светло"). (2026-09-27, Ponytail Lazy Dev; user approved: "fix the problems first, then continue".)
    - **Scope (from `resources/General_reviews/PETRIN_SVET_MASTER_EXECUTION_ROADMAP.md` §"Task GAME-PIANO-001 — Make free play primary", lines 1507-1557).** Keep: immediate sound on touch, free play, per-key visual feedback, song learning mode. Change the song UI to the "Прати светло" visual phrase system: the expected key **softly** lights; a correct press plays the note + a brief positive highlight + advances the indicator; a wrong press must NOT produce strong negative feedback but gently re-indicates the expected key. Establish the song data structure the roadmap prescribes (`{ id, title, notes: ['C4',...], tempo, speech }`) so local Serbian children's songs can be added later without a refactor. Acceptance criterion: **free play stays more prominent than challenge mode.**
    - **Current gaps confirmed by reading the code (2026-09-27):** song data is `notes: [0,0,4,…]` (bare keyboard indices) + a parallel `rhythm` array — not the roadmap shape, and note names/octaves/tempo are absent, so adding a Serbian children's song later means re-deriving everything. The song button is labelled "Свирај песму" with no "Прати светло" phrasing. The expected key gets a **hard** highlight (`.lit` = full `--kcolor` fill + `translateY(-2vmin)`), not a soft light. A wrong press currently calls `window.gentleMiss()` + `.shake` + red "Покушај још једном!" — exactly the "strong negative feedback" the roadmap says to remove. Free play is the default mode but both mode buttons are visually identical pills, so the acceptance criterion is not observable.
    - **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-27 — `game/games/piano.js`, `game/pages/piano.html`.** (1) **"Прати светло" song mode**: the mode button is now literally `Прати светло` (aria-label "Прати светло — свирај песму тако што пратиш светло"); the expected key gets a **soft light** — a resting glow plus a slow pulse in the key's own colour, no hard colour swap, and the note letter picks up the key colour. (2) **Correct press** = note + brief green `.good` outline + the indicator (`#pianoCounter`, now `aria-live="polite"`) advances. (3) **Wrong press never punishes**: `gentleMiss()`, the `.shake` animation and the red "Покушај још једном!" are all gone; the light simply pulses again on the expected key (`.hint`) with a neutral "Светли ти овде 🎵" in `--plum-soft` (no more red). The smoke counts `gentleMiss` calls to prove it is never invoked. (4) **Song data on the roadmap shape** `{ id, title, notes:['C4',…], tempo, speech }` with a `NOTE_INDEX` map and a `hold` array (per-note length in beats, so melodies keep their phrasing; `tempo: 500` reproduces the previous `rhythm[i] * 0.5` timing exactly) — a Serbian children's song can now be added as one array, with no engine change. `speech` is spoken on song entry/chip select so a child who cannot read the title still hears which song it is. (5) **Free play is visibly primary** (roadmap acceptance criterion): `#modeFree` is "Свирај слободно" at 5.4vmin vs "Прати светло" at 3.2vmin, and the smoke asserts the computed font sizes.
    - **BUG FOUND + FIXED during verification — 3 of 8 keys were unreachable on a phone (pre-existing, found via Playwright at 390×844).** `accessibility.css:2-3` puts a 64px `min-width` on *every* `button`; 8 keys need ~545px but `#pianoStage` is ~367px on a 390px viewport, and `#app{overflow:hidden}` clipped the overflow — the top three keys (including high C) were off-screen and untappable, which breaks "free play first" on the project's primary device. Fixed locally in `piano.html` with `#pianoKeys .piano-key{ min-width:0 }` — the global a11y floor is untouched, and the keys are 133px tall so the tap target is still ~5.6k px².
    - **BUG FOUND + FIXED during verification — the soft light was invisible on any motion-enabled machine (mine).** I first put the glow only inside `@keyframes softLight`; but `playNote` leaves the one-shot `.hit` class on the key forever, and `.piano-key.hit{animation:keyPop}` comes later in the stylesheet, so it shadowed the `.lit` animation and a played key showed **no** glow at all. Fixed twice over: the resting glow is now declared on `.piano-key.lit` itself (so it is a plain declaration, not something an animation can take away) and `playNote` removes `.hit` after 350ms. Both are pinned by smoke checks. The reduced-motion branch is now just `animation:none` — the task-83 trap (the user's OS reports `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, so headless does too, and the first version of the check was measuring the static fallback) still keeps the glow.
    - **Validation:** `node tools/run_all.js --game piano_smoke` → **22 checks, ALL PASS** (15 before). Full ritual `node tools/check_all.js --docs --offline` → **ALL 19 TOOLS PASS, 0 fail**; `docs/` re-synced (209 files); offline pack rebuilt (`sw-cache-list.json` 208 entries, `offline-manifest.json` 208 entries, `docs/game-offline.zip`). Layout verified over HTTP with Playwright at 390×844 (no overlap between title, mode buttons, `#songInfo` and the keyboard; no page overflow). NOT committed (user commits/pushes).
    - **Note:** this model cannot read images, so the visual look of the new soft light was verified through computed styles and DOM geometry, not by eye. Screenshot review needs MiMo V2.5 Free.

- 105. DONE — Play-test round 6 + chatGPT_review2 mechanical fixes (racing3d + hub). (2026-09-25, Ponytail Lazy Dev; user play-test on a real device + supplied `resources/3dracer analysis/chatGPT_review2.md`. **user approved "proceed with all batches" 2026-09-25**.) All four reported defects were reproduced with a headless probe before being fixed; the steering choice was delegated to me and resolved as *remove the nose-point, keep the bank*.
   - **(1) racing3d hub button was off-screen → fixed.** Root cause: `#hub-games .hub-grid` had `margin: 24vh auto 0` and `minmax(9rem,1fr)` only fits 2 columns on a 390px-tall screen, so the 5-row grid could not fit at any button size (measured `bottom` 639 in a 390px viewport; only 4-5px of clearance on tablet). Added a `@media (max-height: 560px)` block: `margin: 14vh auto 0`, `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(6rem,1fr))`, smaller `padding-bottom` and `.hub-btn` clamp. Verified 3 columns and **all 10 buttons inside the viewport** on 844×390 (`racing3dBottom` 379/390); tablet sizes unaffected.
   - **(2) steering zones swallowed the 🌍/🔊 buttons → fixed.** `.r3d-zone` is `z-index: 11` at `top: 26vmin` (=101px on 844×390) while `.r3d-corner` was `z-index: 10` at `top: 118px`, so `elementFromPoint()` at both button centres returned `r3d-zone-right`. Raised `.r3d-corner` to `z-index: 20` (the same value `#r3d-back` already used) — the review's own recommendation. Both buttons are now the topmost element at all three tested viewports. The 🏠 back button needed no change (already `z-index: 20`).
   - **(3) back button had no arrow → fixed.** `racing3d.html` `#r3d-back` was an empty `<button>`; it now carries the same inline Material arrow SVG as driving/ocean/space/dino. The retired `racing.html` back button is left alone with the rest of task 96.
   - **(4) steering arrows were two different emoji glyphs → fixed.** `⬅`/`➡` replaced with mirrored inline SVG arrows (`M20 11H7.83…` / `M4 11h12.17…`), styled `.r3d-zone .r3d-btn svg { width/height: 58% }` and the emoji `text-shadow` removed, so both render identically on every platform.
   - **Steering rotation — user delegated the choice; resolved as "remove the point, keep the bank".** Deleted the whole-body nose yaw (`kartLean.rotation.y = slipYawValue` plus the now-dead `slip`/`slipYawValue`/`lateralVel`/`prevLateral` chain — the read as "the car spins sideways"). The bank was reduced **0.34 → 0.22 rad** (19.5° → 12.6°) instead of removed, and the front wheels still turn at full lock so the car clearly shows where it is going. Lateral travel (`lateral += steerDrive * LATERAL_GAIN * dt`) is unchanged, so handling is identical. Round 3 (user-requested) and round 5 (user-complained) are both recorded; **do not re-add the body yaw**.
   - **Review queue (all applied):** `spawnP()` now stores `spin` (5 call sites passed it and `updateParticles` read `p.spin`, so nothing ever spun) and the cap guard is `>=` so the pool cannot reach 421; context-loss string finished as "Графика се привремено искључила. Сачекај…"; `play_matrix.mjs` DEFAULT_PAGES now includes `/pages/racing3d.html`; `resetInput()` added (clears `keys` + `zonePointer` + all smoothed steer state) and called on `launch()` and on `blur`/`visibilitychange`; reduced motion now also gates kart suspension bob, camera steer-sway, camera roll, offroad rumble shake and the boost FOV punch — the speed-linked FOV widening is deliberately **kept** (it is a speed cue, and a constant FOV feels slower); dead `ACCEL = 16` replaced by the real `ACCEL_RATE = 2.2` used by the speed update.
   - **Compound audio motif spacing (the 40 ms drain fix).** The bus drains one item per 40 ms, so the second note of a motif was released late and its own `delay` was added on top — the pickup fifth sounded like a flam and the 100 ms minor third stretched to 140 ms+. Added `qMotif()`, which queues the notes as separate items (the drain rate is deliberate, and the smoke pins the queue counts) but anchors them to the moment the **first** note actually played. Both the pickup fifth and the obstacle "uh-oh" now use it; the kind string is `tone,speak,motif,motif` and that smoke expectation was updated to observed truth.
   - **Perf instrumentation (review item 8, "measure before optimizing").** Added dev/test-only `__r3d.perf()` (draw calls, triangles, particle count, live rolling average frame ms, worst frame ms in a 120-frame window, DPR, draw-buffer px) and `resetPerf()`; sampling uses unclamped wall-clock spacing, skips halted/context-lost frames, and nothing in the game reads the numbers. `tools/headless.js` gained an optional `dpr` (default 1, no behavior change) so a test can compare DPR 1 vs 2. **Measured under this harness's software GL (NOT a device verdict):** tablet 1180×820 DPR1 drive = 71 calls / 13.5k tris / 17.6 ms avg / 24.2 ms worst; the same view during a confetti+celebrate burst = **162 calls** (≈ +1 call per particle, confirming the review's hypothesis) / 29.6 ms worst; phone 844×390 = 100 calls / 16.7 ms; DPR2 = 3.87M draw-buffer px and an 83.6 ms worst frame, i.e. 4× the pixel cost — but the average never filled a window under software rasterization, so DPR2 is inconclusive here.
   - **Regression guards added (the fixes are now pinned by tests, not just fixed):** `racing3d_smoke.js` 27 → **32 checks** (SVG arrows + non-empty back button; corner buttons topmost mid-race at 844×390; particles carry non-zero spin; `resetInput` clears on demand and on `blur`; perf hooks sane — asserted as hook wiring only, never as an FPS threshold, which would be flaky). `hub_smoke.js` 9 → **10 checks** (every visible games button inside an 844×390 viewport, racing3d included). New `__r3d` hooks: `particlesSpinning`, `inputState`, `resetInput`, `reducedMotion`, `perf`, `resetPerf`.
   - **Validation:** `node --check` clean; `racing3d_smoke.js` **32/32 PASS**, `hub_smoke.js` **10/10 PASS**; `node tools/play_matrix.mjs racing3d` → **10/10 cells OK** (chromium + webkit × 5 viewports, first time racing3d has been in the matrix); `node tools/check_all.js --docs --offline` → **all 19 tools green (racing3d 32 checks)**, `docs/` re-synced (201 files), offline manifest rebuilt (200 entries) + `docs/game-offline.zip`. NOT committed — user commits/pushes.
   - **DECIDED 2026-09-25 (user delegated both calls): do NOT pool particle materials.** Measured cost is ≈+1 draw call per particle (71 → 162 in a confetti burst) and the burst frame time did not degrade beyond noise (17.6ms avg / 29.6ms worst, software GL). Sharing one material per particle class would break the **per-particle fade** at `racing3d.mjs:1520` (`p.m.material.opacity` is set per mesh), so every confetti flake in a burst would fade in lockstep — a visible regression traded for draw calls that the measurement shows cause no problem. The review itself said "first measure; do not optimize blindly", and the measurement says there is nothing to fix. Revisit only if a real device shows burst frame-time spikes.
   - **DECIDED 2026-09-25 (user delegated): KEEP the reduced-motion gates as they are — do not restore the camera motion for the user's own machine.** Three reasons: (1) the user has only ever complained about *excess* motion (round 5, the sideways spin) and never asked for camera bounce; (2) every gate is behind `prefers-reduced-motion`, so users who do not set it still get the full chase-cam juice — honouring the OS preference costs them nothing and is not overridden for looks; (3) the user has been play-testing with reduced motion ON the whole time (the harness reports it ON too, `decorAmp === 0`) and kept giving feedback rather than reporting a dead-feeling game, which is evidence the gated build still plays well.
   - **Note on the user's own machine:** the user runs with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` ON, and the smoke harness does too, so **every reduced-motion gate above is what the user actually play-tested** — the game as shipped to them is the fully-gated build. See the decision above: that is the intended state, not a divergence to fix.
   - **Decisions closed by the user (2026-09-25):** task 96 (2D racer) is **RETIRED**; the real-device iOS check from batch 11 is **provisionally closed** (no iOS testing planned — the audio unlock stays as a no-op-safe mitigation).

- 104. DONE — Accessibility: allow pinch-zoom site-wide + fix the coloring progress contrast. (2026-09-25, Ponytail Lazy Dev; **user approved 2026-09-25**.) Two root causes, both single-value fixes:
   - **Pinch-zoom was disabled site-wide (critical WCAG 1.4.4).** 15 of 18 pages carried `maximum-scale=1, user-scalable=no` in the viewport meta (3 spelling variants). Removed both tokens; every page now keeps only `width=device-width, initial-scale=1[, viewport-fit=cover]`. DONE — Offline download self-healing: the hub "Преузми ванмрежно" cache-all run can hang forever on a single file (per-file `cache.add()` has no timeout in `game/sw.js:88`), so the percentage sticks and the user must restart the app (then it rushes through the already-cached files and stops at the same place). Desired behavior (2026-09-22, user chose "Timeout + retry + skip"): per-file 20 s timeout, retry ×2, then **skip the stuck file and keep going**; the completion message reports how many files were skipped and a button press retries them. (2026-09-22, user request.)
   - Implemented (2026-09-22, Ponytail Lazy Dev): `game/sw.js` — new `addWithTimeout(cache, url, timeoutMs=20000, retries=2)` (AbortController + retry, returns false on final failure) replaces the bare `await cache.add(url)` inside `cacheAllAssets`; skipped files are collected and sent on the `cache-complete` message (`skipped[]`). `game/shared/navigation.js` — completion status now reads "Сачувано за ванмрежни рад (N прескочено — покушајте поново)" when any files were skipped (retry = press the button again; cached files re-fetch fast, so a re-run only really costs the missing ones). No new dependencies. Validation: `node --check` clean on both files; `node tools/hub_smoke.js` ALL PASS (0 fail); `tools/sync-docs.sh` (201 files); offline package rebuilt (`tools/build_offline.ps1` → sw-cache-list 200 entries, new zip with the fixed sw.js). - 102. DONE — Racing3d polish round 2: external AI review combined plan (11 micro-batches). (Started 2026-09-22 batch 1 per user "start doing the items one by one"; full plan in `resources/3dracer analysis/combined_action_plan.md`.) Racer review was run through ChatGPT/Claude/Grok/DeepSeek (folder `resources/3dracer analysis/`, 4 reviews + RACER3D_ANALYSIS.md); this task turns the combined, deduped recommendations into implementation items, one micro-batch each, ordered by impact/cost. **User-locked decisions (2026-09-22):** (a) simultaneous left+right steer = **most-recent-wins**; (b) resume after backgrounding = **clean auto-resume** (no overlay; suspend Clock+AudioContext while hidden, discard one stale delta); (c) **contact/blob shadows INCLUDED** (user-requested, overrides Grok's defer). Ground-truth note: the reviews' feared `x *= 0.85` per-frame decay does NOT exist — easings use `Math.min(1, k*dt)` (racing3d.mjs:1416/1419/1425/1563), so Batch 1 is normalization + a `__r3d.step(dt)` test hook, not a bug fix. Boost-FOV kick already exists (60→80, +6°). Batches:
   - **Batch 1** — frame-timing normalization (`1-exp(-k*dt)` on 4 sites) + seconds-based audit (ambient/beep/music) + `__r3d.lastDt`/`step(dt)` 60↔120 Hz smoke case. (P0, all 4 AIs.)
   - **Batch 2** — touch/palm hardening: per-zone `pointerId` ownership + capture + `pointercancel`, `touch-action:none`, ignore outside-zone touches, most-recent-wins. (P0, all 4 AIs.)
   - **Batch 3** — visibility pause + audio suspend/resume (clean; locked decision). (P0, all 4 AIs.)
   - **Batch 4** — reactive decor billboards: kart-proximity scale-pop/tilt/bob on the 16 emoji sprites + gate/flags wave, `REDUCED_MOTION`-gated amplitude. (P1, all 4 AIs' top "wow".)
   - **Batch 5** — contact/blob shadows (shared radial-gradient sprite, ~30 sprites: kart/pickups/obstacles/boost pads). (P1, DeepSeek; user-requested.)
   - **Batch 6** — boost visual identity: recolor/intensify dust emitter during boost + longer flames + soft rear-wheel road trail. (P1, Claude + Grok.)
   - **Batch 7** — pickup feedback: kart emissive flash on collect + rising chime pitch per consecutive pickup (audio-only, reset on obstacle). (P1, Grok + DeepSeek.)
   - **Batch 8** — celebration choreography: lap-complete + finish world-colored petal/emoji bursts reusing weather emitters (cap 420). (P1, ChatGPT + Grok + DeepSeek.)
   - **Batch 9** — audio: 4 `GainNode` buses (`music/ambient/sfx/speech`) + ducking 6–10 dB + progressive obstacle tone (<30 u) + beep rate-limit. (P2, all 4 AIs.)
   - **Batch 10** — minimal spoken Serbian hint: «Пази!»/«Браво!», first-per-session + 8–10 s cooldown, Serbian-voice check silent fallback, beep stays authoritative, gated behind audio toggle. (P2, all 4 AIs.)
   - **Batch 11 DONE (2026-09-25, Ponytail Lazy Dev, user approved)** — manual/dev QA of the four P2 items. **(1) WebGL context-loss:** audited `racing3d.mjs` and the vendored three.js (`three.module.min.js` registers `webglcontextlost`/`webglcontextrestored`, skips rendering while lost and re-inits the GL context), so *restore* was already sound — the real gap was that a GPU reset showed the child a frozen black screen while the sim kept running, then teleported on restore. Added a `contextLost` flag, a Serbian overlay ("Графика се привремено…") on loss, sim freeze in `loop()` (with `clock.getDelta()` drain so `dt` doesn't spike on resume), a `showCountdown()` early-return so the countdown can't stomp the overlay, and `setSize` + `onShow()` on restore. **(2) iOS audio-unlock synchronicity:** a CDP probe (trusted `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, since a synthetic `dispatchEvent` is *not* a user gesture) showed `main.js:2`'s first-pointerdown unlock works, but the `state` transition is asynchronous — still `suspended` 400 ms after the gesture on `index.html`/`racing3d.html`, settling ~200-400 ms later, so `tone()` was scheduling against a frozen `currentTime`. Added the standard iOS silent-buffer unlock to `audio.js` (`createBuffer(1,1,22050)` played once inside `ctx()`) plus a `.catch` on `resume()`. Verified as a safe no-op on desktop Chromium (state unchanged). Real-device iOS verification still needs a physical device. **(3) Offline delivery matrix — found and fixed a real shipping bug:** `game/offline-manifest.json` was committed **empty (`{}`)** while `sw-cache-list.json` listed 200 assets, so the service worker's update check (`sw.js:43`) compared against nothing and `docs/game-offline.zip` shipped a useless manifest. Root cause: `check_all.js:90` spawns `powershell` (Windows PowerShell 5.1) and the child inherits a `PSModulePath` that cannot auto-load `Microsoft.PowerShell.Utility`, so **`Get-FileHash` was not a recognized cmdlet** — all 200 hashes silently failed, and under `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest` the aborted loop left `$manifest` empty, which the script then wrote out while still reporting success. Fix: hash via the BCL (`[System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()`) instead of the cmdlet, `-LiteralPath` throughout, a 5-try backoff retry for transient locks, and two hard guards — refuse to write an empty manifest, and exit non-zero if any entry fails. Also added superseded-cache cleanup to `sw.js` `activate` (bumping `CACHE_NAME` previously orphaned the whole previous offline copy on a device forever). Verified: 200/200 entries, sha256+size on all, hashes cross-checked against Node `crypto` for `sw.js`/`index.html`/`racing3d.mjs`/`cat.ogg` (all match), all 52 html/js/mjs/css files covered. **(4) `?debug=1`:** audit result — nothing to gate. `racing3d.mjs` emits **zero** `console.*` calls, and the `window.__r3d` hook is intentionally always-on because `tools/racing3d_smoke.js:14` loads `/pages/racing3d.html` with no query string and check 1 asserts `__r3d` exists; gating it on `?debug=1` would break 27 passing checks. Left as-is deliberately. Validation: `node tools/check_all.js --docs --offline` → Syntax OK (63 files) + **ALL 19 TOOLS PASS (361 checks)** + docs synced (201) + **offline manifest 200 entries**; `node tools/play_matrix.mjs` → 50/50; `node tools/axe_check.js --all --report` → 17/17 clean. **Item (2) iOS real-device verification PROVISIONALLY CLOSED by user 2026-09-25** ("we can provisionaly close, i will not be testing on IOS") — the silent-buffer unlock in `audio.js` stays as-is (verified a safe desktop no-op); do not reopen unless the user starts testing iOS. Task 94 — detailed plan & subtasks

Goal: Add a simple, secure, child-friendly way for caregivers to download and install the full game (all pages + assets) to an Android tablet so the game runs offline and receives easy updates. Preferred UX: PWA (service worker) that caches assets on-demand + a prebuilt ZIP fallback for manual installs.

Subtasks (small, verifiable, surgical):
1) Audit assets & generate file list — DONE (2026-08-07 18:25:59 +02:00)
1.5) Ensure per-page start functions exported for standalone boot — DONE (2026-08-07 20:12:00)
   - Files updated: game/games/animals.js, shapes.js, candy.js, coloring.js; added window.startX exports so shared/main.js reliably boots standalone pages.
   - Validation: node --check passed; headless smoke targeted re-runs recommended on user approval.

   - Script: tools/generate_sw_list.js (node) — enumerates all files under game/ and outputs game/sw-cache-list.json. (Created)
   - Validate: game/sw-cache-list.json created with 183 entries (includes index.html, all pages/, games/, shared/, assets/). Path: game/sw-cache-list.json.

2) Web App Manifest + icons — DONE (2026-08-07, Radman Milos)
   - Create game/manifest.json (sr-cyr) and add icons to game/assets/icons/ (192 + 512). Provide fallback PNGs if originals too large.
   - Validate: manifest references icons and start_url '/game/index.html'.
   - Status: created manifest.json with Serbian Cyrillic metadata, `start_url: /game/index.html`, `scope: /game/`, and valid PNG icon entries `icon-192.png` and `icon-512.png`.
   - Icon adaptation: the user-provided transparent download-button PNGs were copied to the required manifest icon names; the original `download-*.png` files remain for the hub button. SVG variants remain as development/source alternatives. (2026-08-07)

3) Service worker (game/sw.js)
   - Status: DONE (2026-08-07, Radman Milos) — audited and hardened the existing worker.
   - Implement install/activate/fetch handlers using CACHE_NAME with semantic versioning (petrin-v1).
   - Implement message handler supporting {cmd: 'cacheAll'} and progress events via postMessage.
   - Implement update check: compare content-hash list / cache manifest and expose {cmd:'checkForUpdates'} returning changed count.
   - Safety: do not cache huge runtime-build-only files (tools/) or node_modules.
   - Result: same-origin `/game/` fetch restriction added; update comparison now includes removed files; cache list regenerated with 193 runtime entries and no tools/node_modules paths. `node --check game/sw.js` and `node tools/hub_smoke.js` pass.
4) Registration & UI
   - Status: DONE (2026-08-07, Radman Milos) — hub manifest link, worker registration, download progress, update check, and ZIP fallback link integrated.
   - Register SW in shared/navigation.js (graceful fallback when SW unsupported).
   - Add a simple, prominent hub button: "Преузми ванмрежно" (id=download-offline) with states: Idle → Caching (progress %) → Ready (Installed) → Update available (Ажурирај).
   - Add an unobtrusive link to game-offline.zip (prebuilt) as a fallback.

6) Update & versioning UX

   - SW must use CACHE_NAME with version string; implement skipWaiting/clients.claim and prompt users with 'Ажурирај' when new version available.

12) Acceptance criteria
   - Status: DONE (2026-08-07, Radman Milos) — implementation, automated acceptance checks, manifest/cache/ZIP checks, and regression smokes complete. Physical Android Chrome/WebView confirmation remains a manual follow-up.
   - Validation: `docs/game-offline.zip` contains `index.html`, `manifest.json`, `sw.js`, both PNG icons, and `docs/OFFLINE_INSTALL.md`; package size 5,338,546 bytes. No commit/push performed.
   - A caregiver can tap the hub 'Преузми ванмрежно' button while online and the site caches all assets; the game loads offline and audio/speech play correctly.
   - Updating the game on the server prompts a visible, simple update flow on-device.
   - No existing game functionality regresses; all smoke tests pass.

Rules (MANDATORY)
- Manifest: game/manifest.json must exist with start_url '/game/index.html', scope '/game/', lang 'sr', and icons at game/assets/icons/icon-192.png and icon-512.png. Use Serbian Cyrillic fields (name/short_name/description).
- Docs & approvals: after completing EACH subtask, update PROJECT_TASKS.md, HANDOVER_PROMPT.md, and README.md as appropriate, run tools/sync-docs.sh, and ask the user for explicit approval to continue. Do NOT commit or push without the user's explicit instruction.
- Build prompt rule (NEW): after any task is marked DONE, the assistant MUST ask the project owner whether they want to build a new/updated offline download package (tools/build_offline.ps1). This confirmation must be recorded in the task log and the user's explicit approval is required before running the build script or creating game-offline.zip.
- Non-regression: changes must be purely additive and must not alter existing game runtime behavior. Any change that could affect gameplay requires targeted smoke tests and the user's approval before committing.
- Child-friendly install: the PWA install flow must be a single-tap caregiver action with clear Serbian instructions; updates must be simple and transparent (background download + single-button apply). The hub "Преузми ванмрежно" button will also act as an "Ажурирај" button when a newer offline package/manifest is detected; pressing it will download and apply updated assets.

Notes & constraints
- Do not change game runtime behavior. All changes must be additive and reversible.
- Avoid adding large new dependencies; use built-in browser APIs + small node/ps scripts for build-time tooling.
- Ensure service worker scope is correct (served from / or /game/ depending on hosting). If site hosted under docs/ on GitHub Pages with root at /, choose sw scope accordingly.

Owner & timeline
- Owner: Radman Milos (you). I can implement after you confirm and after these doc updates are accepted. Estimated work: 1 working day to implement + 0.5 day for testing/polish.
